# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

<meta>catchword: he</meta>

## Upon the Eleventh Emblem.

### *Concerning the Tree of Christian Faith.*

Human reason <note>original: "Het vernuft" — in this context, the intellectual faculty that relies on logic and sensory evidence, which the author views as limited in spiritual matters.</note>, because it cannot behold God, must be constrained into hope; there, doubt runs against Faith and seeks to disturb hope. Then the earnest Will must struggle against that earthly reason with the right Image <note>original: "Beeltenis" — referring to the *Imago Dei*, the spiritual reflection of God within the human soul that must be restored.</note>. This causes pain, and it often proceeds mournfully, especially when reason beholds the course of this world and thus gives its Will to it, being known as foolish against the course of this world. There it is said: *be sober, watch, fast and pray* <note>A paraphrase of 1 Peter 5:8 and Matthew 26:41.</note>, so that you may dull that earthly reason and make it as if dead, so that God’s Spirit may find a place in you. 

When the Spirit itself appears, He quickly overcomes the earthly reason and looks upon the Will in its anguish with His love and sweetness; there, each time, a beautiful little sprout is born from the tree of Faith. Thus all sorrow and temptations serve the children of God for the best: for as often as God permits His children to be led into anguish and sorrow, they stand each time in the birth of a new sprout from the tree of Faith. When the Spirit of God appears again, He raises a new growth each time, over which that noble Image rejoices very highly. It is only about weathering the first storm, where the earthly tree must be overcome and that noble grain must be sown in God’s field, so that man may learn to know the earthly man; for when the Will of God receives light, the mirror sees itself in itself, one power in the light sees the other: thus the whole man finds himself in himself, and knows what 

<summary>This page provides a mystical commentary on the struggle between human reason and spiritual faith, comparing the growth of faith to a tree that sprouts anew through trials and divine intervention.</summary>

<keywords>Faith, human reason, the Will, spiritual growth, divine light, trials, the Tree of Faith, Imago Dei</keywords>

--- Page 65 ---
<meta>This page continues the prose commentary on the "Tree of Christian Faith" from Emblem XI. The previous page ended mid-sentence, discussing how the Spirit’s appearance is not about the outward person but about the inner reality of God.</meta>

# THE FIRST PART.

D 3

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

...[who] he is, which he cannot recognize in the earthly intellect <note>original: *aardſe vernuft* — the logical, worldly mind that relies on sensory evidence rather than spiritual intuition.</note>.

Thus no one should think that the tree of Christian Faith is seen or known in the kingdom of this world; the outward intellect does not know it. And even if that beautiful tree stands beautifully within the inner man, the earthly intellect still doubts; for the Spirit of God is like foolishness to it, and it cannot comprehend it. Although it may happen that the Holy Spirit opens Himself in the outward mirror <note>A metaphorical reference to the way divine light reflects in our external perception or emotions.</note>, so that the outward life rejoices greatly therein and trembles with great joy, thinking: "Now I have received the worthy guest, now I will believe," yet there is no perfect constancy in this. For the Spirit of God does not always remain there in the earthly well of the outward life; He desires a pure vessel, and when He withdraws inward into the birth of light, as into that true Image <note>original: *Beeltenis* — the *Imago Dei*, or the divine image within the human soul that the believer seeks to restore.</note>, then that outward life becomes faint-hearted and despondent. Therefore the noble Image must always be in conflict and live against that outward intellect; the more it struggles, the larger the beautiful tree grows, for it works with God. For just as an earthly tree grows in wind, rain, cold, and heat; so also does the tree of the image of God grow under the cross <note>To live "under the cross" is a historical phrase meaning to endure suffering or trials for the sake of one's faith.</note> and sorrow, in anguish and affliction, in mockery and contempt, and grows up in God’s Kingdom, and brings forth fruit in patience.

JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### The Soul keeping herself only to Christ.

Though a darkness, bred by cunning and strife,
Has covered the world's Pilgrim's path, so heavy and dismal,
In which mist, neither on this nor on that side,
Were there false lights <note>original: *Dwaallichten* — literally "wandering lights" or will-o'-the-wisps; a metaphor for false doctrines or worldly distractions that lead the soul astray.</note> that often lead the Pilgrim astray,
Yet I have good courage and hope, because I trust,
If I keep my eyes straight and simply <note>original: *recht en slecht* — in this period, "slecht" meant "simple" or "plain," rather than "bad." It implies a single-minded, humble focus.</note> on JESUS,
And set my feet exactly in the prints of His feet,
And step forward in His light, so that with good peace,
I shall yet finally come to the place I desire,
In the true Fatherland <note>A common spiritual term for Heaven, the soul's original home.</note> of the strayed human:
Though sometimes a terrible monster comes wandering there,
And strikes me with a deathly fear; and drenches 
->my hair<-
in sweat, the closer I hold to JESUS, all my salvation;
And though the narrow way is generally somewhat sharp and steep;
Thickly set with thorns, which flesh and blood <note>A biblical idiom referring to human nature in its frail, unregenerate state.</note>
->fear,<-
The sweeter shall the rest be in the other life.

## Divine Answer.

I am the light of the world: he who follows me shall *not walk in darkness, but shall have the light of life. John VIII: 12.*
*Walk while you have the light, so that the darkness does not overtake you. And he who walks in the darkness does not know where he goes.*
*While you have the light, believe in the light, so that you may be children of light. John XII: 35, 36.*

<meta>This page continues the meditation on Emblem XII from Jan Luyken’s "Jesus and the Soul." While the previous page provided the poetic verse and scriptural foundation (Psalm 119), this page offers the prose commentary, emphasizing the practical imitation of Christ’s life as the only way to navigate a confusing and divided world.</meta>

JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### On the XII. Emblem.

->*Regarding the safest way to travel through the dark world into the eternal Fatherland.*<-

The Lord says: *Whoever follows me, does not walk in darkness.* <note>John 8:12. This opening paragraph closely mirrors the start of Thomas à Kempis's *The Imitation of Christ*, a cornerstone of 17th-century Dutch spirituality.</note> These are Christ’s words, by which we are admonished that we must follow His life and conduct if we truly wish to be enlightened and delivered from all blindness of heart. Therefore, let our highest diligence be to meditate upon the life of Christ. Christ’s teaching surpasses the teaching of all the Saints: and those people who have the Spirit of God find the <term>hidden Heavenly bread</term> <note>A reference to "Manna" or spiritual sustenance that nourishes the inner soul rather than the physical body.</note> within it. But it often happens that many people feel little desire or fervor from the constant hearing of the Gospel <note>original: *Euangeliums*</note>, because they do not possess the Spirit of Christ. But whoever wishes to understand Christ’s words rightly and completely must strive to conform his entire life to that of Christ.

O poor, perplexed Soul, standing in the midst of the manifold divisions of this warring and envious world, where one cries "here" and another "there," <note>A common Pietist critique of the sectarian infighting between various Protestant denominations of the era.</note> not knowing where you shall turn in this darkness; keep your eyes upon the Lamb of God, follow close at His heels, and set your feet in His most holy footsteps; learn from Him gentleness and humility <note>original: *ootmoedigheid*</note>; learn from Him love, kindness, obedience, and patience <note>original: *lydzaamheid* — also implies long-suffering or endurance</note> unto death. Forsake all things along with your "self," take up your cross, and follow Him through a constant death of your desires in true poverty; for these things you have no need for the light of a <term>learned Doctor</term> <note>original: *geleerden Doctors* — referring to academic theologians. The author suggests that simple, sincere devotion is superior to complex scholarship.</note>, as long as your will is in truth inclined toward God alone.

A good-hearted person asked a true friend of God,

<summary>This page provides a prose reflection on the necessity of imitating Christ's character to find spiritual clarity. It argues that internal devotion and the "Spirit of Christ" are more important for salvation than academic theology or sectarian debates.</summary>
<keywords>Imitation of Christ, spiritual darkness, Lamb of God, humility, inner light, Jan Luyken, Pietism, sectarianism</keywords>

--- Page 69 ---
<meta>This page continues the prose commentary for Emblem XII. The previous page ended mid-sentence, advising the reader to set their feet in the "most holy footsteps" of Christ. This page concludes that thought by identifying Christ's suffering and the "death of nature" as the only true path to spiritual rest.</meta>

THE FIRST PART.

...of God, what might be the most pure <note>original: *allerloutersten*</note> way to reach the ultimate goal? To which he answered: the nearest and surest way to this is the suffering of our Lord JESUS CHRIST, that man should bear the same within himself with great gratitude and love, and contemplate himself within it, so that man observes with all diligence in what way he still lives deficiently, and in what things his life is unlike the life of Christ—what still affects him with pleasure and sorrow; he shall, out of love, <term>die away</term> <note>original: *affterven*. A central concept in 17th-century Dutch mysticism referring to "mortification," or the process of the soul dying to worldly desires to be reborn in God.</note> to those same defects, offering the high-worthy death of Christ as a recompense, and abandoning his own will and desire, inwardly and outwardly.

This pleases God above all things, which he demands and desires from man. Whoever walked this Way, the Lord would without any doubt advance him into the very nearest and most hidden ways, in his most secret, special, inward, and most blessed splendor; in which no one must place himself before he has first walked this path; through this dying of nature, inwardly and outwardly, given to spirit and nature.

Whoever, in this dying, goes out of himself in the most profound and truthful way, in that same person the eternal Truth reveals itself most nakedly and clearly, and he is led into the **hidden darkness**: into that silence, into the **deep abyss** <note>In the tradition of "negative theology" (like that of Meister Eckhart), the "abyss" or "darkness" represents the presence of God which is so bright it appears dark to the human intellect.</note>, where the true rest is in God.

Why do we run so long back and forth, seeking a way where we might enter into the kingdom of heaven with our own will, with the <term>old Adam</term> <note>A biblical reference to the fallen, sinful nature of humanity that must be "put off" to become a "new man" in Christ.</note>? As long as we fear death, we shall remain deprived of the true life; a continuous death is the right way to God and eternal life.

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>This page describes the mystical "Way" of suffering and self-denial, explaining that the only path to divine rest is through "dying" to one's own will and imitating Christ’s life.</summary>

<meta>This page marks a transition from the prose commentary found on page 57 to a new poetic section, likely Emblem XIII. While the previous page discussed the mystical "dying of nature" and the "deep abyss," this page uses the metaphor of a rower struggling against a current to illustrate the soul’s active persistence in the spiritual life.</meta>

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### *The Soul steadily rowing against the current of sins.*

Although I am tired and worn out from rowing,
On this stream of sinful desires,
Which steadily flows downwards,
Yet I do not think to wait, nor to rest;
For if I did that for a short time,
I would see my work frustrated and lost,
We would always remain just as far away,
And never come to the place where we belong.
O **Wise Steersman** <note>original: *Wyze Stierman*. A common 17th-century metaphor for Jesus as the pilot of the soul's ship.</note> give me strength,
And keep the rudder of life in your hands,
While we row day and night,
Through wind and current, until we land,
At the little corner where we must be;
Let the completed work rest sweet and safe,
Too early a rest breeds mourning and pain,
And much sorrow, which we should never desire.
Row on, my spirit, row on with courage,
Steady work will one day come to an end;
After the bitter, one best tastes the sweet,
Joy is most beautiful after sadness and misery.
Row on, my spirit, row on with strength,
Until you have completed the journey here.

### Divine Answer.

He that shall endure unto the end, the same shall be saved.
**Matthew 24:13.**

<meta>catchword: A</meta>

<note>Vocabulary from the original: soul, stream, sins, rowing, Steersman, Divine Answer, Matt. 24:13</note>

<header>JESUS AND THE SOUL.</header>
<meta>catchword: ar-</meta>

## On the XIII. Emblem.

### *Concerning the flowing stream of Sins, and how the Soul must row against it.*

The world is a flowing stream, which with all its pleasures, desires, prosperity, and adversity, constantly drifts downwards everything that sails upon it. Upon this stream, the Soul finds herself in the old boat of **corrupt flesh and blood** <note>original: *verdorven vlees en bloed*. A theological term referring to human nature in its fallen state, susceptible to sin.</note>; there she is commanded to stand watch, to row without ceasing against this strongly flowing river, if she ever desires to reach the corner of true rest and peace. But oh! what sweat and terrors it costs! What many a storm blows over her! How often does a dark sky appear, pregnant with lightning and thunder! How many times does she strike a hidden reef! What rain showers fall upon her, and how wet she sometimes becomes from the crashing waves; yet she takes comfort in all this through the firm hope of a very good and beneficial end. And sometimes there also comes a particularly beautiful and friendly sunshine, by which she is refreshed and takes new courage; meanwhile, the **Lord** holds the rudder in his hands and preserves her from destruction: for without the **governance of God** <note>original: *bestieringe Gods*. Divine providence or the way God manages and guides the universe and individual lives.</note>, all her toil and

<summary>This page presents a prose meditation on the soul's journey, comparing the world to a treacherous river where the soul must row against the current of sin. It describes the various hardships of spiritual life as storms and reefs, while positioning God as the essential navigator at the helm.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual navigation, worldliness, divine providence, perseverance, metaphor of the sea, human nature, salvation</keywords>

THE FIRST PART. 61

labor is lost; but so long as she diligently rows with the oars of **divine desires**, prayer, and supplication, and faithfully applies her strength, the **divine guidance** <note>original: *goddelyke bestieringe*. This refers to God’s active management and providence over the soul's journey.</note> shall not depart from her, until He has brought her into a safe harbor, where an eternal rest is prepared for her.

But whoever is now too listless or too slow to row upon this **stream of the world**, is continually and without ceasing driven downwards; and while he stares in idle wonderment at the swimming of the fish, the flying of the birds, and at the pleasant and delightful banks, trees, flowers, mountains, and buildings—without being concerned as to where he will eventually land—he thus reaches the end, and takes a terrifying fall into the **abyss of Hell**.

Oh! that we were all wise, and directed ourselves toward that end for which the good God has created us. Oh! how much better it is to labor here and rest eternally hereafter, than to neglect our time here and hereafter be separated from God, from all that is good.

The greatest and most profound distress of all that the damned shall suffer is this: namely, that they are separated from God. For whoever shall feel what it is to be separated from God, shall feel **hellish pain**. God is essentially all that is good; and there is no good but God, and he who is deprived of such, has nothing. Therefore it is said: *he is poor who travels to Hell.*

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

62

**The Scoffer** <note>original: *Spotter*. A person who mocks or doubts spiritual truths; a common figure in 17th-century religious dialogues.</note> says: I have indeed heard much of **Hell**, but I never saw anyone who had been there, nor do I know any path in the whole world that leads there. Oh! I have certainly seen someone who has been there, and you yourself walk upon that path the whole day long. If one **born blind** said to you: I have indeed heard much of the night, but I have never seen it, would you therefore doubt whether there was also a night? Yes, even if the whole land were full of such blind people, and you alone were sighted, you would still not doubt it. You are blind to **eternity**. **Hell** is present everywhere; if, in dying, the light of the Sun leaves you, and you do not have the **light of God** shining within you, then you stand in the midst of **eternal darkness**.

<summary>This page refutes the skeptic's claim that Hell is a distant or non-existent place by using the metaphor of a blind person doubting the existence of night. It argues that Hell is a state of eternal darkness that exists wherever the light of God is absent.</summary>

<keywords>The Scoffer, Hell, spiritual blindness, eternity, light of God, eternal darkness</keywords>

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

->*The **Soul**, resting from all external multiplicities <note>original: *menigvuldigheden*. In mystical tradition, this refers to the distracting variety of worldly thoughts and sensory inputs that prevent focus on the "One" (God).</note>, keeps watch with the internal eye of the mind.*<-

Though I seem dead to the world,
And cut off from [earthly] life,
Oh no! oh no! there is no need for fear;
I sleep in my external limbs,
I rest from all earthly business,
From all worldly luxuries
And pleasure, for the animal body,
From multiplicity and images <note>original: *beelden*. This refers to mental representations or sensory distractions that occupy the mind during meditation.</note>;
Thus I sleep, but my heart wakes,
Upon God and Divine things,
So that when the Lord approaches me,
He does not find me slumbering.
I sleep, but the eye of my mind <note>original: *gemoed*. A term for the inner spirit, encompassing the heart, will, and intellect.</note>
Is always open and uplifted,
And beholds in God the eternal good,
The eternal light, and eternal life.
Oh! yes, my flesh just sleeps on,
And sinks even deeper into the forgetting
Of all that does not belong in the heart,
And that the Spirit does not need to know.
Ah! if only the world were dead with me,
In a forgetfulness of sins!
All of humanity would be deified <note>original: *vergood*. This refers to the concept of *theosis*, where the soul is transformed by grace into a god-like state of union with the Divine.</note>,
And the true life would be found.

<meta>This page continues the commentary on Emblem XIV, expanding on the spiritual discipline of "vigilance." JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### On the XIV. Emblem.

#### Concerning the Vigilance of the Spirit.

I slept, but my heart was awake. Song of Songs 5:2.

I rest from worldly delights, now that the senses have sufficiently fallen asleep; but I desire those things to come, and I seek eternal things; thus I sleep, and thus I watch.

The righteous person sleeps regarding evil and all the lusts of the flesh; but he watches in the virtues.

The flesh must sleep, faith must watch: the desires of the body must sleep, and the prudence of the heart must watch.

The Saints despise all earthly things and entirely abandon the bustle of the world, not because they are lazy or sluggish, but because they work inwardly and apply themselves to behold in the heart what they are and why they were created; for they do not sleep because they are tired, but they rest from perishable things so that they might more freely contemplate the eternal. Sleep is an image <note>original: *evenbeeld*. A likeness or mirror-image.</note> of death, for through sleep all activity of the senses in the body ceases, and man enters into a forgetfulness of all cares; fear is dampened, anger laid down, and the perception <term>bevindelykheid: the state of feeling or experiencing through the senses</term> of all sorrow is cut off. From this we learn that the one who says she sleeps with the body and watches with the mind <term>gemoed: the inner spirit, encompassing the heart, will, and soul's disposition</term> has been raised above herself; for in those in whom the mind alone lives, and who are not grieved by any sensory awareness, the bodily nature is sufficiently overcome by a sleep, and the sight is truly closed by a rest: Then the eye

<summary>A prose meditation on the mystical state of being "asleep to the world but awake to God," explaining how the withdrawal from sensory distractions allows the soul to contemplate eternal truths.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual vigilance, Song of Songs, mortification of the senses, inner life, the Saints, contemplation, sleep as image of death</keywords>

of the Souls free and unbound, and shows itself with distinction. It contemplates only those things that are higher than the visible: at that time the hearing is effectively dead and perished, as it has lost its function. But the mind <term>gemoed: the inner spirit or disposition of the heart</term> occupies itself with things that far surpass the intellect <term>vernuft: the faculty of human reason or ingenuity</term>.

The powers with which the Soul works are just like chambermaids <note>original: *Kamenieren*. Female attendants or ladies-in-waiting; a metaphor for the soul's natural faculties serving the higher spirit.</note>, who lead her into that highest part of herself; before the bedchamber of her eternal King. And when the Soul with her powers is lifted up, into the highest above all created things, and is kindly embraced by her beloved, then the powers must withdraw and rest from all activities, and the Soul is flooded by the Spirit of God, and blessedness <term>zaligheid: the state of spiritual bliss or salvation</term> is worked in many, yes, in a thousandfold ways; and then she feels in the loving power a pull <note>original: *trek*. A spiritual attraction or magnetic "drawing" of the soul toward the divine.</note> of the Holy Spirit; like a living fountain, which flows with rivers of eternal sweetness.

When the Soul comes so high into the light that the understanding <term>verstand: the rational or intellectual faculty</term> is blinded, just as the eye is by the brightness of the Sun, so she receives above the intellectual power a simple eye, opened in the activity of the loving power; which eye, with a simple contemplation, sees in that spiritual clarity all that God is in His simplicity <note>original: *eenvuldiglyk*. In the mystical tradition, this refers to Divine Simplicity—the idea that God is without parts or complexity, perceived only by a soul that has become "simple" or single-minded.</note>. But what then happens to the spirit of man, and what he recognizes at that time, cannot be expressed with words, nor does he himself fully recognize it when he returns to himself.

<meta>This page continues the exploration of the soul’s mystical journey. -> JESUS AND THE SOUL. <-

### *The enlightened Soul speaks of how, or in what manner, God forgives sins.*

The dreadful darkness is not taken away,
As long as the morning light waits to arise.
Do not deceive yourself, O man! you are not rid of sins,
As long as you are still the **old Adam** <term>old Adam: a theological term referring to the fallen, sinful nature of humanity before spiritual rebirth</term>.
It is all a deception: neither praying nor pleading helps,
If you do not wish to break your **false will** <term>false will: the self-centered ego or desire that resists divine guidance</term>;
Let whoever wishes comfort you, it is lying language and false,
You keep the burden of all your sins upon your neck;
When Christ rises in the heart, then the night of sins,
And all darkness, is swallowed up in the divine light:
And if that does not essentially happen in this time <note>original: *deezer tyd*. Referring to one's earthly life.</note>,
Then your poor Soul remains a night in eternity.
It does not happen there as you may have heard here,
That a criminal man, through a King’s words,
Was forgiven his guilt out of favor, while he did not become better,
But remained a rogue, a thief, or murderer in his heart,
No, it must go differently: a burning fire of the Lord,
Must consume the **old evil man** to dust and ashes,
From that a life greens <note>original: *groend*. A poetic way of saying "sprouts" or "flourishes," like a new plant growing from the ashes of the old.</note> forth, which God loves in Christ,
Unburdened of all guilt and adopted as His child.

## Divine Answer.

Wash yourselves, purify yourselves, put away the evil of your doings from before my eyes, cease to do evil. Learn to do good, etc. Come then, and let us reason together, says the Lord: *Though your sins were as scarlet, they shall become white as snow; though they were red as crimson, they shall become as [white] wool.*
Isaiah 1: 16, 17, 18.

<meta>This page continues the theological reflection on Emblem XV, moving from the poetic verse of the previous page to a prose explanation. It emphasizes that forgiveness is not a legal transaction but a complete internal transformation—a "new birth"—contrasting true spiritual change with a superficial "historical" belief.</meta>

-> JESUS AND THE SOUL. <-

## On the XV. Emblem.

### *On the Forgiveness of Sins.*

When Christ rises, then Adam dies. When the Sun rises, the night is swallowed up into the day, and there is no more night; such is the forgiveness of sins.

O seeking and longing mind, that hungers and thirsts for the Kingdom of God, take note of the foundation being shown to you: It is not such a light thing to become a child of God as people think, where one carries their conscience only in **histories** <term>histories: a term used by mystics to describe a purely intellectual or factual knowledge of the Bible that lacks personal, spiritual experience</term>, and thus tickles oneself with Christ’s suffering and death; where one learns of the forgiveness of sins "historically," like a worldly court where someone’s debt is forgiven out of grace even though they remain a rogue in their heart. It is quite different here; God wants no hypocrites. He does not take sins away from us in such a way if we only cling to the intellectual knowledge and comfort ourselves with Christ’s suffering while our conscience remains mired in abominations. It is said: you must be born again, or you shall not enter the Kingdom of God. 

If someone wants to tickle themselves with Christ’s suffering and death and claim it for themselves, and yet wishes to remain with their own will un-reborn in the **Adamic man** <term>Adamic man: the "old self" or fallen human nature that remains centered on worldly desires rather than God</term>, they act just like someone who comforts themselves with the thought that his Lord will grant him his land, despite the fact that he is not his Son, even though the Lord has promised it only to his Son. So it is here as well: if you want to possess your Lord’s land and have it as your own property, you must become his true Son; for the son of the bondwoman shall not inherit with the free <note>A reference to Galatians 4:30, contrasting the "child of the flesh" with the "child of the promise."</note>. The "son of histories" is a stranger; you must be born out of God in Christ, so that you [may be] a...

<meta>catchword: beloved-</meta>

<summary>This page argues that divine forgiveness requires a literal spiritual rebirth rather than just an intellectual acceptance of biblical facts. It warns that merely "tickling" oneself with the story of Christ's sacrifice without changing one's inner nature is a spiritual delusion.</summary>
<keywords>forgiveness of sins, spiritual rebirth, New Birth, Adamic man, historical faith, Kingdom of God, Jan Luyken, inner transformation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the prose explanation of Emblem XV. It picks up mid-sentence from the previous page, completing the thought that one must truly become a child of God through spiritual rebirth to inherit Christ's glory, rather than just knowing the "history" of it.</meta>

# THE SECOND PART.

...become a beloved Son, then you are God’s child, and an heir to the suffering and death of Christ. Christ’s death is your death, his resurrection from the grave is your resurrection, his ascension is your ascension, and his eternal Kingdom is your Kingdom; if you are born as his true Son from his flesh and blood, then you are an heir to all his goods; otherwise, you cannot be an heir as Christ's child.

As long as that earthly kingdom remains within your **Image** <note>original: *Beeltenis*; in this mystical context, it refers to the inner form or "statue" of the soul which should reflect God but is currently occupied by worldly desires</note>, you are the earthly son of the corrupt **Adam** <note>referring to the "Old Adam," the fallen state of humanity</note>: hypocrisy will not help there. Give as many sweet words before God as you wish, yet you remain a stranger’s child, and God’s goods do not belong to you, until you come back to the Father with the **prodigal son** <note>A reference to the parable in Luke 15:11–32 regarding the return of a wayward child to their father</note>, with true repentance and real penance for your lost inheritance. There you must exit the earthly life with the **willing spirit** <term>willing spirit: the faculty of the soul that directs human desire and intent toward either the world or God</term>, and break the earthly will—which is a painful process—abandoning with the mind and the willing spirit the precious treasures for which that spirit was born, and you must enter into God’s will. There you sow your seed in God’s Kingdom, and are born anew in God, like a fruit that grows in God's field, for your will receives God’s power; Christ’s spiritual body—understand this as a heavenly essence that fills the eternal depth—and from that, a new spiritual body grows for you in God. This body remains hidden within the coarse earthly body for the duration of this life, just like **gold in a stone** <note>a favorite metaphor of the author, suggesting that the divine nature is a precious substance encased in the "ore" of our physical, fallen humanity</note>; then you are God’s child, and Christ’s possessions belong to you.

### *The Soul in contemplation of the new Creature.*

When the little seed died in the earth’s womb,
And seemed forgotten and lost,
Then the little flower grew green through that death,
And came forth as if born anew,
Out of dark earth into the beautiful light,
To offer up fragrance and colors.
Greeted by the face of heaven,
With dew and sunshine at dawning;
So grows the new creature <note>original: *nieuwe kreatuur*; a reference to 2 Corinthians 5:17, "if any man be in Christ, he is a new creature," meaning a soul transformed by grace</note>,
When one's own will goes into destruction,
In death and the grave, though it tastes bitter,
A life turns green through that dying;
A flower in Paradise so beautiful,
Though those born blind to the world see it not,
He stands before God and His Son,
And is fragrant through all the angels' choirs.
O beloved JESUS! O eternal good!
How fervently I desire to become
A little flower on your rose-garland <term>rose-garland: *roozenhoed*, literally "rose-hat," referring to a crown or wreath of flowers, a common mystical symbol for a state of blessedness or the company of the saints</term>,
Where never a leaf withered!
O Lord JESUS, lead me on,
To always enter into death <note>referring to the mystical "death of the self" or "mortification," where the believer dies to worldly desires to live for God</note>.

<meta>This page continues the exploration of Emblem XVI. The previous page concluded with a poem about the "new creature" emerging from the death of the self. This page provides the prose commentary or "Contemplation" that explains the mystical and theological foundations of that rebirth, specifically focusing on the "water of life" and the internal nature of the spiritual path.</meta>

JEZUS AND THE SOUL.

->On the XVI. Emblem.<-

->*Of the Rebirth or the New Creature.*<-

**J**ust as a beautiful flower rises up from the black earth—from the death and the decay of the seed into another element, the air, made clear by the light of the Sun—so the new creature <note>original: *nieuwe kreatuur*; referring to the spiritually reborn person as described in 2 Corinthians 5:17</note>, the true image of God, grows out of the dark depths, from the death of the old Adam <note>original: *den ouden Adam*; a theological term for the fallen, sinful nature of humanity that must be "put off" to achieve spiritual life</note>, not for outward eyes, nor in this world, but in the unique, pure element of God that is within.

And just as the flower sucks into itself water from which its being grows, so the soul that has turned <note>original: *omgewende Ziele*; a soul that has undergone conversion, turning its focus from the material world toward the divine</note> sucks into itself that water of life through its desire; and from this grows that highly esteemed, noble, and exceedingly beautiful image: the new creature. You must not think of this water as being merely a parable, for it is a substantial water; yet you shall not compare it to this external, mortal water, nor think that it is a river or flowing brook that exists here or there in a particular place. Oh no! That water of life is that heavenly substance <note>original: *wezentheid*; a term used in mystical theology to describe the "essential reality" or "being" of the divine</note>, born of the gentleness of the Majesty of God, and this substance, the water of life, fills the entire eternal depth of the infinite Godhead. Thus, when Christ invites us to the water of life, we do not need to go here or there, below or above, but we shall—through this outward world, breaking through with our thirsty desire—enter into that inward realm where God is omnipresent; there, this water of life is in all places.

The right way into eternal life is within man; he has led the Soul’s will into this outward world, and he must now lead it back into himself, into that inward spiritual world where God is everywhere present.

<summary>This page uses the metaphor of a flower growing from a decaying seed to explain spiritual rebirth, emphasizing that the "water of life" is a literal divine substance found within the soul rather than a physical location.</summary>
<keywords>rebirth, new creature, old Adam, water of life, divine substance, omnipresence, inwardness, conversion, Jan Luyken</keywords>

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>This page continues the prose commentary regarding the rebirth of the soul. The previous page ended mid-sentence, describing the "water of life" as a heavenly substance born of the gentleness of God’s majesty, intended to nourish the soul.</meta>

<header>THE SECOND PART. 75</header>

...ish, and eat **spiritual bread**, and drink **spiritual water**, from which the **new man** <note>original: *nieuwe mensch*; the spiritually transformed person as described in the New Testament</note> increases and takes on a form.

But he has a dangerous and difficult path to walk; for that **turned-about will** <note>original: *omgekeerde wille*; a will that has been converted or turned away from worldly desires toward God</note> must enter back into itself, and must break through that **realm of stars and elements** <note>original: *sterren en elementen ryk*; a term from Jacob Boehme’s mysticism referring to the material and astral world that surrounds and influences the soul</note>: oh, how he is held there! There must be a great earnestness, no hypocrisy, and [no desire] to remain with the will in the star-realm, in this outward world.

Alas! In this net of outward luxury most people remain hanging, ensnared by their corrupt lusts and false cravings, and they do not break through to that inward, to that glorious **Kingdom of God**; and because they do not wish to **die** <note>original: *afsterven*; the process of mortification, or "dying to the self," necessary for spiritual resurrection</note> to that old life, they can never obtain the new.

In a **reborn** person, one sees no outward change; the shape and stature of the body is just as before, and for that reason the coarse world—which wants everything to match its own crudeness—regards it as smoke and wind; but because the **new birth** is not of this world, it can neither be understood nor grasped by this world.

But the **new man** is inward, in the **deep ground** <note>original: *diepen grond*; a mystical term referring to the innermost essence or "spark" of the soul</note>; there it grows essentially, hidden within the old, just as gold grows within the coarse stone.

If your eyes were opened at the time when the outward flesh and blood falls away through death, then you would see this **new creature**, that glorious and beyond measure beautiful image, standing like a highly adorned flower, turned green and fully grown through death, in that joy-filled **Paradise of God**.

### On the 16th Emblem.

#### *On Rebirth or the New Creature.*

Just as a beautiful flower grows up from the black earth, out of the death and the rotting of the seed into another element—the air, illuminated by the light of the Sun—so the **new creature**, the true image of God, grows out of the dark coarseness <note>original: *grotheid*; refers to the density or "grossness" of material, fallen existence</note>, out of the death of the **old Adam** <note>the fallen state of humanity inherited from Adam, representing the sinful nature</note>, not for outward eyes, nor in this world, but in the only pure element of God that is inward.

And just as the flower sucks in a water from which its essence grows, so the **turned-about Soul** <note>original: *omgewende Ziele*; a soul that has undergone conversion, turning from the world toward God</note> sucks that **water of life** into itself with its desire; and from this grows that high-valued, noble, and exceedingly beautiful image: the **new creature**. You must not think of this water as being merely a metaphor, but it is **substantial water** <term>substantial water: in mystical theology, this is not physical water, but a real, spiritual "matter" or "heavenly corporeity" that nourishes the soul</term>; yet you shall also not compare it to this outward, mortal water, nor think that it is a river or flowing brook that exists here or there in a specific place. Oh no! That **water of life** is that **heavenly substantiality** <note>original: *hemelse wezentheid*; a term often used in Behmenist mysticism to describe the "stuff" of heaven</note>, born of the gentleness of the Majesty of God; and this substantiality, the water of life, fills the entire eternal depth of the infinite **Godhead**. Thus, when **Christ** invites us to the water of life, we do not need to go here or there, below or above, but we shall—through this outward world—press our thirsty desire into that inward world where God is everywhere-present; there, this water of life is in all places.

The right way into eternal life is within man; he has led the will of the Soul into this outward world, and he must now lead it back into himself, into that inward spiritual world where God is everywhere present, to lead...

But because the **new birth** is not of this world, it can neither be understood nor comprehended by this world.

If your eyes were opened at the time when the external flesh and blood fall away through death, then you would see this **new creature**—that glorious and immeasurably beautiful image—standing like a highly adorned flower, grown green and fully bloomed through death, in that joyful **Paradise of God**.

## On the 16th Emblem.

### *Of Rebirth or the New Creature.*

Just as a beautiful flower grows up from the black earth, out of the death and the rotting of the seed, into another element—the air, illuminated by the light of the Sun—so the **new creature**, the true image of God, grows out of the dark coarseness, out of the death of the **old Adam** <note>In Christian mysticism, "the old Adam" represents the fallen, sinful nature of humanity that must "die" to allow spiritual rebirth.</note>, not before external eyes, nor in this world, but in the only pure element of God that is inward.

And just as the flower sucks a water into itself from which its essence grows, so the **turned-about Soul** <note>original: *omgewende Ziele*; refers to a soul that has turned its will away from worldly desires and back toward its divine source.</note> sucks that **water of life** into itself with its desire; and from that, there grows within it 그 noble, highly valued, and exceedingly beautiful image: the **new creature**. You must not think of this water as being merely a parable; it is a substantial water. Yet, you should not compare it to this external, mortal water, nor should you think it is a river or flowing brook that exists in one specific place or another. Oh no! That **water of life** is that heavenly essence, born of the gentleness of the Majesty of God; and this essence—the water of life—fills the entire eternal depth of the infinite Godhead. Thus, when **Christ** invites us to the water of life, we do not need to go here or there, below or above; rather, we shall break through this outward realm, leading our thirsty desire into that inward realm where God is omnipresent. There, this water of life is found in all places.

The right way into eternal life is within man. He has led the Soul's will out into this external world; he must now lead it back into himself, into that internal spiritual world where God is omnipresent.

<header>JESUS AND THE SOUL.</header>

## *The Soul Refutes the Old Adam.*

<note>In mystical tradition, "the old Adam" represents the unredeemed human nature, driven by physical appetites and worldly logic, as opposed to the "new man" born of the spirit.</note>

The old man, that coarse beast,
Possessed by a starry spirit <term>starry spirit: a term from Jacob Böhme’s theosophy referring to the "astral" influence of the physical cosmos that governs the natural, unredeemed human mind</term>,
Who never reached a deeper foundation
Than the mother who made him <note>Likely referring to the material nature or "Mother Earth" from which the physical body is formed.</note>,
Spoke to my soul with boldness:
"Where then is that beautiful heaven,
For which you forgo all joy
And sink into a constant dying?" <note>This "dying" refers to the mystical practice of *mortification*, where the seeker dies to their own ego and worldly desires to find life in God.</note>
So asks a man who never saw:
"Where is the Sun? Where is the day?"
It is you yourself, oh man of sins,
Who keeps our eyes bound;
If you were purely gone with all your filth,
We would see God and the Kingdom of Heaven,
And all the angels around us.
That is no delusion; he knows it,
Who once, by favor of the Eternal Good,
Opened an eye within his soul.
Therefore, I desire to leave you;
As long as I live, I will hate you,
And cut off all your desires
Until you fall away from me in the grave.

JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### On the XVII. Emblem.

->*Of Paradise, where it is, and how one*<-
->*may behold it.*<-

Reason <term>reason: from the Dutch "het vernuft," referring here to the human intellect or rational mind which relies on sensory evidence rather than spiritual revelation</term>, which went out of Paradise with Adam, asks: where is that Paradise to be found? Is it far or near? Is it in this world, or outside the space of this world above the stars? Where does God then dwell with the Angels? And where is that dear fatherland, where there is no death? Since no sun or stars are within it, it must not be in this world, otherwise it would have been found long ago.

Dear reason, no one can lend another a key <note>original: "fleutel," an archaic spelling or OCR variant of "sleutel" (key)</note> to it, and even if it were so that someone had a key to it, he still does not unlock <note>original: "fluit," corresponding to "sluit" (shuts or locks)</note> it for another; each must unlock <note>original: "opfluiten," corresponding to "opsluiten" (to lock up/unlock)</note> it with his own key, otherwise he does not enter: for the key is the Holy Spirit; when one has that key, he goes in and out. <note>The text notes that the words 'fleutel', 'fluit', and 'opfluiten' appear with an 'f' but contextually function as 'sleutel' (key), 'sluit' (shuts/unlocks), and 'opsluiten' (to unlock/lock up).</note>

Nothing is closer to you than Heaven, Paradise, and Hell; whichever you are inclined toward, that is what you are closest to at this time. You are between the two, and there is a birth <term>birth: "geboorte," a term in mystical theology (often associated with Jacob Böhme) referring to the internal realization or "bringing forth" of a spiritual state within the soul</term> between each one; you stand in this world in both doors, and have both births within you. God holds you in one gate and calls you; and the Devil holds you in the other gate and calls you also; whichever you

These sluggish outward eyes cannot see it, for they are from the most outward **BIRTH** <term>Birth: "Geboorte," in this mystical context, refers to the different planes of existence or "principles" that manifest within a person. But the sluggish body cannot enter therein for that reason, nor does it belong there; it belongs to the earth, and must rot, and in new power—which is like Paradise—rise in Christ at the end of days; then it may also dwell in Paradise, and not before; it must first lay aside that third, most external **BIRTH**, like this coarse garment <note>original: "ruuwen rok," literally "rough coat," a reference to the physical body or the "coats of skins" given to Adam and Eve after the Fall.</note>, in which father Adam and mother Eve fell asleep, and in which they imagined they would become wise.

## On the XVIII. Emblem.

### On the Omnipresence and Nearness of God.

Moses speaks: *The Lord our God is one God:* <note>original: "De Heere onze God is een eenigen God," a reference to the Shema in Deuteronomy 6:4</note> And in another place it is written: *From him, through him, and in him are all things.* <note>original: "Van hem, door hem, en in hem zyn alle dingen," a reference to Romans 11:36</note> *Do I not fill all things?* <note>original: "Ben ik niet die alle dingen vervuld?," a reference to Jeremiah 23:24</note> Through his Word all things were made that are made. Therefore one must say that he is the origin of all things; he is that eternal immeasurable Unity. Consider this example: if I think of what would remain in the place of this world if the four elements <note>Earth, air, fire, and water—the fundamental building blocks of the universe in pre-modern science</note> along with the stars and nature were to disappear and cease to be, so that there was no longer any nature or creature? The answer: There would remain that same eternal Unity, from which nature and creation received their origin. Likewise, when I think of what lies many hundred thousand miles above the stars, or what exists in the place where there is no creature? It is that eternal, unchangeable Unity, which is that unique good, having nothing behind it or before it that gives it anything or takes anything away, or from which this unity arose; there is no ground, time, nor place, and it is the only God, or 그hat unique good, that cannot be expressed in words.

Thus we understand that in the eternal depth there can be no place, even if it were as small as a mustard seed, where God would not be essentially present; and so he is also in my Soul,

<summary>This page offers a theological reflection on God's omnipresence, describing God as an eternal, immeasurable "Unity" that exists beyond time, space, and the physical elements, yet resides intimately within the human soul.</summary>

O Soul, yes indeed, and I am greatly overjoyed by this, that my God and my Father is so near to me and within me. O my God, my ground <note>In mystical theology, the "ground" (Dutch: *grond*) refers to the deepest, most essential part of the soul where the human meets the Divine.</note>, how many times in my foolishness have I looked with an outstretched neck toward the starry heaven, and you were so near! O Jesus, my fairest beloved, now I no longer need to seek you many thousands of miles from here, for where your Father is, there you are also. And because you are born from all the powers of your eternal, immeasurable Father, in all places, in the endless depths of eternity, from eternity to eternity, so you are also born in the circumference of my Soul, if it but makes itself empty of the coarseness <note>referring to worldly attachments, sins, or material distractions</note> that it had sucked in through the false will, lust, and desire; and thus opens the door, then you, O knocking Jesus, enter in, and flow through the entire Soul with the eternal light of life, which you yourself are. O my fairest beloved, it pleases me to speak yet longer of your presence, for you yourself say: *I and my Father will make our dwelling with the one who loves me. John 14:23.* Now, where you and your eternal Father, O fairest Beloved! are present, there the Holy Spirit also proceeds from you both, just as it is spoken: *Do you not know that you are temples of the Holy Spirit, who dwells in you,* so then all divine power, according to its eternal birth, is in the ground of my Soul like a living fountain, if I love you rightly, O sweetest Jesus, and leaving all things, faithfully follow you.

A water like crystal, upon which no breeze plays,
Receives the sun's image so proudly and beautifully;
So was the beautiful Soul, the noblest of all things,
Which through the wisdom of God received an essence;
A spark of the eternal fire, shone through with God's light,
A clear Mirror for the eternal countenance;
Where the eternal, endless One—neither in height nor breadth,
Nor eternal depths, never to be fathomed nor measured—
Beheld Himself, and found in a created image,
That which had played in His wisdom before the creation. <note>This refers to the mystical idea that the "idea" of the soul existed in Divine Wisdom (Sophia) before the physical world was made.</note>
That clear Mirror has been violated by false desire;
That crystalline vessel filled with the coarseness of sins;
That still, pure ground <note>In mystical tradition, the "ground" (Dutch: *grond*) is the deepest part of the soul where man and God are one.</note> moved out of its harmony,
And the noble Image so miserably disturbed.
What shall man do now? What paths should he best take
To receive this lost Image back into the heart?
A Will, proceeding from the eternal Soul-fire,
That drives, as with a whip, the entire creature, <note>In this context, "creature" (*kreatuur*) refers to the worldly, animalistic nature of man that must be disciplined.</note>
With all its images out, and stands still before the Godhead;
Stripped of all earthly inclination, so that He fulfills His will.
There is no other counsel, though it cost woe and pain,
It must be suffered, or it must be lost.

> Ephes: IV: 22, 23, 24.

JESUS AND THE SOUL. II

## On the XIX. Emblem.

### *Of the Soul, what she essentially is.*

God is a pure Being, from whom all things receive their essence. My essence flows without mediation <note>The term "without mediation" (Dutch: *zonder middel*) refers to the mystical belief that the soul's core is directly connected to God without any intervening priest, ritual, or physical substance.</note> from God, and from the essence flows life, and from life blessedness. Gather yourself and all the powers of the Soul into that inward part of the Soul—that is, that essence—and stand still; then God must necessarily shine in.

My Soul is a mirror, in which God shines and gives light at all hours; but that I do not fully perceive His light in my Soul, I myself am the cause, because I do not withhold myself from all created things, and do not lay my Soul bare; she is too often multiplied <note>In mystical theology, "multiplicity" refers to being distracted by the many things of the physical world, whereas "oneness" refers to focus on God.</note> with outward things, and hindered so that she cannot see God within herself.

If the mirror is held before me, my form and image must be reflected in the mirror, whether I will it or not: so also, if man empties himself of all outward things, and all powers are gathered into the essence, and man comes into a forgetting of himself and all things, then God must necessarily shine and gaze into the Soul, and such a gaze is better than the whole world with all its goods. In a mirror a face is imaged, and the whole form, but the nature and the essence goes

It calls for a state of "self-forgetting" and internal stillness to allow God's image to be reflected in the soul's essence.</summary>

not into it. But if God images Himself in the Soul,
then nature and essence go into it. In short, that
divine nature pours itself entirely into that bare
essence of the Soul, if she is pure and clean. My
face with shape and form indeed pours itself into

lay myself still under God, and let God be everything in me,

and right conversion, and that eternal life.

<meta>This page transitions from the abstract concept of God's essence filling the soul to a more visceral metaphor: the soul must "eat" the divine essence just as the body eats food. It continues the previous page’s discussion on how God and the soul interpenetrate, moving from the idea of the mirror to the idea of nourishment and the birth of the "new man."</meta>

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.
<meta>catchword: Faith-</meta>

->*The Soul desires to have Christ, the bread of life, essentially within her.*<-

Even if I saw the whole house filled with food,
And that I, like cattle in the grass, lay therein,
I would still remain unsatisfied; it would provide me no nourishment;
The food must be *within* me, if I were to live.
Now notice, O child of man, this certainly concerns your condition:
Though the eternal depth is full of the clear Godhead, <note>This "eternal depth" refers to the omnipresence of God throughout the universe, which surrounds the soul like air or water.</note>
As long as the soul-fire <note>The "soul-fire" (*zielenvuur*) is a mystical term for the innate longing or the spiritual spark within the human heart.</note> does not eat of the divine essence,
So long shall it also be a dry hunger.
Just as from the nourishment that the seed draws into itself,
A spirited little flower is brought forth into its body;
So is the fiery soul covered with renewal,
If she has truly soaked in the essence of God;
That holy essence becomes so glorious around the spirit,
A beautiful and spiritual body of heavenly flesh and blood,
Therein she burns eternally, and from her consuming yields
A light, clear Spirit, a mirror for the Lord.
This is the new man, <note>The "new man" is a concept from the New Testament (Ephesians 4:24) representing the spiritually reborn self.</note> who is hidden within the old,
Like gold in a stone; when the old breaks,
Then the new stands revealed, before God and the choirs of angels;
Thus, through true faith, the true man is born.
True faith does not merely remain standing outside:
But breaks through everything, and takes hold of the Godhead.
That must be her food, and give her strength and wisdom;
That is her bread and wine, and nourishment for the true life.

Having established the soul's desperate hunger in the previous section (illustrated by the pantry and the thirsty deer), the author now explains that Christ must be an internal, living reality rather than an external concept. The metaphor shifts from the pantry to the biological necessity of a tree's sap and a nursing child's milk to illustrate "essential" union with the divine.</meta>

JEZUS AND THE SOUL.

<meta>catchword: in</meta>

## On the XX. Emblem.

### *How we must have Christ essentially within us.*

All moisture that is from the outside cannot help the tree, but the moisture that it draws into itself gives it life, growth, and fruitfulness.
Even if the whole world were full of Christ, and Christ were not in me or did not come into me, then it could not help me; I would be and remain a withered and fruitless tree.
This is what a Christian is: one who, with their inward ground <note>original: "inwendigen grond" — a mystical term referring to the deepest, most essential part of the human soul where it meets the divine.</note>, heart, and will, has turned toward the granted grace in Christ Jesus. In the will of their soul, they have become like a young child who only yearns for its mother's breasts, who thirsts for the mother and sucks from the mother's breast, from which it lives.
In the same way, only that person is a Christian whose soul and mind enter back into that first mother (from whom human life sprouted, namely that eternal WORD <note>original: "WOORD" — referring to the *Logos*, the divine Word or Reason of God as described in the Gospel of John.</note>, which has revealed itself with the true milk of salvation in our humanity, which is blind to God). They drink this motherly milk into their hungry soul, from which the new spiritual humanity arises.
For only he is a Christian in whom Christ dwells, lives, and exists; in whom Christ has arisen and become living according to the soul’s inward ground. Such a person has "put on" <note>An allusion to the biblical concept of "clothing" oneself in Christ's attributes.</note> Christ's victory over God's wrath, as well as over hell, the devil, death, and sin (such as Christ's humanity, suffering, death, and resurrection) within their inward ground. There, the seed of the woman <note>A traditional theological reference to the Promised Savior who would defeat evil.</note>, as Christ in His victory,

<vocab>Emblem, Christ, Soul, WORD, Christian, essentially, humanity</vocab>

<summary>The author explains that true Christianity requires an internal, "essential" connection to Christ rather than mere external knowledge, using metaphors of a tree's sap and a nursing infant. He argues that the soul must "drink" the divine Word to birth a new spiritual nature and overcome sin and death.</summary>

<keywords>inner life, spiritual growth, union with Christ, new man, divine nourishment, inward ground, the Word</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the explanation of Emblem XX. On the previous page, the author used metaphors of a tree drawing moisture and a child nursing to argue that Christ must be internal and "essential" to the believer, rather than a distant historical figure. This page concludes that thought, contrasting "imputed grace" (external) with "indwelling grace" (internal transformation).</meta>

# THE SECOND PART.
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

...conquers in him also, and daily crushes the head of the serpent in the will of the wicked flesh, and kills the sinful lust of the flesh. <note>The author refers to the "crushing of the serpent's head," a traditional biblical reference (Genesis 3:15) interpreted here as the internal victory of the soul over its own lower, sinful nature.</note>

For in CHRIST alone are we accepted into divine sonship and as heirs of Christ; not through an outward, foreign appearance of a specific adoption of grace, or through a foreign merit of an "imputed grace" <term>imputed grace: the theological idea that Christ's righteousness is legally credited to a person from the outside.</term> from without: but through a childlike, indwelling, organic <note>original: "geleederlyke" — referring to being joined as members of a body or branches of a vine; an internal, structural connection.</note>, essential grace, wherein the conqueror of death—Christ himself—rises from our death with his life, essence, and power within us. He then rules and works in us, as in a branch on his vine, just as the Scriptures of the Apostles testify throughout.

He is no Christian who comforts himself only with the suffering, death, and satisfaction <note>original: "voldoening" — the theological concept of Christ paying the debt for human sin.</note> of CHRIST, and reckons that to himself as a gift of grace, while yet remaining an unregenerate <note>original: "onwedergebooren" — literally "not born again"; someone who has not undergone a spiritual transformation.</note> wild beast; such a Christian is merely a wicked person. For everyone would gladly be saved by a gift of grace: even the Devil would gladly be an Angel again through a grace accepted from the outside.

But that he should turn back, and become as a child, and be born anew from God’s water of grace, of Love, and the Holy Spirit—that does not suit his taste. Likewise, the title of "Christian" does not suit one who wraps himself in the cloak of Christ's grace but will not enter into childhood and the new birth; nevertheless, Christ says that otherwise he may not see the Kingdom of God.

JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### *The Enlightened Soul explains to the external wit: the departure of a righteous person.*

In what manner or how a Soul,
Who kept God’s commands pure,
After her flesh and blood fell away,
Ascends to heaven from this earth?
So asks the old child of Adam, <note>Adam's child: a reference to fallen humanity, representing the natural person who relies on worldly reason rather than spiritual insight.</note>
Who has lost all wisdom,
As blind to God and the Fatherland <note>Fatherland: in this mystical context, it refers to the soul's true home in the divine realm.</note>
As one born blind here on earth.
When the Soul so completely
Sinks away from herself, and holds herself still,
Then the Godhead becomes manifest,
And dwells in her surrendered will; <term>surrendered will: translated from "gelaaten wille," referring to the mystical concept of *Gelassenheid*—the state of letting go of self-centered desires to be fully receptive to God.</term>
If flesh and blood now fall away from her,
Then she is shone through with God’s light,
Like iron with a hot glow,
That is God’s hand; where else would she go?
She is crowned with glory,
And is herself the Temple and the Heaven,
Where God dwells with His radiance,
And His Spirit with a sweet stirring.
The kingdom of heaven is without measure,
Just as it is above the sun and stars,
So it is also here, where the earth stands;
In eternity, there is neither near nor far.

### Divine Answer.

> Say to the righteous that it shall go well with them; for they shall eat the fruit of their deeds.
> Isaiah 3:10. <note>original: "Jezaias III: 10"</note>

### On the 21st Emblem.

#### How it fares with a pious Soul, in the dying of the Body.

External wit <note>External wit: human reason and intellect that relies only on sensory experience and logic, lacking spiritual illumination.</note> speaks: the Soul, when it separates from the body, does it not then travel into heaven or into hell, just as one enters into a house, or as one enters through a hole into another world!

**Answer.** No, there is no traveling in such a manner, for heaven and hell are omnipresent: it is but an inward turning of the will, either into God's love, or into God's wrath <note>God's wrath: in this mystical tradition, "wrath" is not an emotion but a quality of the divine fire experienced by those who turn away from God's light.</note>, and this happens during the time of the body, of which Paul says: *Our conversation is in heaven.* <note>original: "Onze wandeling is in den hemel" (Philippians 3:20). The Dutch 'wandeling' (walking/conduct) corresponds to the older English 'conversation' meaning one's way of life or citizenship.</note> And Christ also speaks: *My Sheep hear my voice, and I know them, and they follow me, and I give them that eternal life, and no one shall snatch them out of my hand.*

So wit asks: How then does such an entry of the will into heaven occur?

**Answer.** When the will surrenders itself fundamentally to God, it sinks away outside of itself, beyond all ground and place, where God alone is manifest, works and wills; so it becomes to itself a nothing according to its own will: then God works and wills in it, and then God dwells in its surrendered will; through this the Soul is sanctified, so that she comes into the divine rest. When now the body breaks, the Soul is permeated with divine love, and shone through with God's light,

<term>Emblem: a symbolic illustration accompanied by a moral or spiritual lesson.</term> <term>Soul: the immortal essence of a person.</term> <term>Body: the physical, mortal frame.</term> <term>Heaven: the state of being in God's love.</term> <term>Hell: the state of being in God's wrath.</term> <term>Paul: the Apostle Paul.</term> <term>Christ: Jesus Christ.</term> <term>Love: the divine light and grace.</term> <term>Wrath: the dark fire of self-will.</term>

<summary>This page explains that heaven and hell are not distant places but internal states of the soul's will, accessible even while living in the body. It describes the process of "sinking away" from the self to allow God's will to operate within the soul, which prepares it for the moment of physical death.</summary>
<keywords>mysticism, afterlife, surrender of the will, internal heaven, omnipresence of God, spiritual death, sanctification</keywords>

just as fire glows through iron, whereby it <note>Referring to the iron.</note> loses its darkness. That is Christ's hand, where God's love completely wounds the Soul and is within her a shining light and new life; thus she is in heaven, and a temple of the Holy Spirit, and is herself God's heaven, in which he dwells.

External wit <note>External wit: human reason and intellect that relies only on sensory experience and logic, lacking spiritual illumination.</note> speaks: How does it happen then, that the holy Soul in this time cannot perfectly experience such light and such great joy?

**Answer.** That kingdom of heaven is working and experiential <note>The Dutch 'bevindelyk' refers to "experiential" or "heart-felt" religion, a key concept in Dutch Pietism.</note> within the Saints in their faith; they feel God's love in their faith, through which the will surrenders itself into God. But that natural life is clothed with flesh and blood, and stands in the opposition of God's wrath, surrounded by the idle lust of this world, which constantly penetrates that external mortal life. There, on one side the world, on the other side the Devil, and on the third side the curse of God's wrath, in flesh and blood, penetrates and sifts <note>A reference to being "sifted like wheat" (Luke 22:31), representing spiritual testing.</note> that life, through which the Soul often stands in anguish when hell thus presses upon her and wishes to manifest itself in her. Yet she sinks into the hope of God's grace, and stands like a beautiful rose in the midst of thorns, until the kingdom of this world falls away from her in the dying of the body. Only then is she truly manifested in God's love, when nothing hinders her anymore. She must walk this time with Christ in this world; Christ redeems her from her own hell, since he penetrates her with his love, and stands by her in the state of hell, and has changed her hell into heaven.

<meta>This page follows the discussion of the sanctified soul being permeated by God’s light like "fire glows through iron." Here, the Soul presents the dark mirror image: how the wicked soul is similarly permeated, but by the fire of sin and the influence of the "hellish smith."</meta>

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### *The Soul speaks of the state of the wicked.*

But alas! what a difference is this!
The godless man gives his will
Into the hands of the hellish smith, <note>The "hellish smith" is a metaphorical depiction of the Devil or the power of evil that forges and shapes the soul toward destruction.</note>
That evil master keeps him still,
And thrusts his Soul into the burning fire
Of malice, lies, envy, and wrath,
And all else of such a nature
As is born from the kingdom of the abyss.
Those vanities all together,
Knit and firmly bound,
Are made manifest within him;
Now his Soul is a house of sins;
For they permeate the whole mind
With their natural powers,
Just as fire does to iron. <note>This directly inverts the metaphor from the previous page; while the holy soul is transformed by the fire of God's love, the wicked soul is saturated by the "heat" of its own vices.</note>
O Man! too great to be so little esteemed,
How you run into your own sorrow,
Into eternal mourning and misery;
That is not what the good Father desires,
He did not create you for such an end.

## Divine Answer.

> Woe to the wicked! it shall go ill with [him]:
> for the reward of his hands shall be given him.
> Isaiah 3: 11.

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### On the 22nd Emblem.

->*How it fares with a godless Soul when the body dies away from her.*<-

How does it go then with the godless?
Answer: This godless Soul does not wish, in this lifetime, to enter into the divine resignation <term>Gelaatenheid: A central concept in Dutch and German mysticism (Gelassenheit), referring to the total surrender of one's own will and ego to God.</term> of her will, but goes continually into her own lust and desire, into vanity and falsehood, and into the devil's will: she takes only malice, lies, pride, greed, envy, and wrath into herself, and gives her will over to them: that very vanity becomes manifest and active within her, and permeates the Soul entirely, just as fire does to iron. This Soul cannot come to divine rest, for God's wrath is manifest within her: and as the body now separates from the Soul, so eternal regret and despair begin; for she finds that she has become entirely such an anxious horror, and is ashamed that she should try to press toward God with her false will; indeed, she cannot, for she is caught in the fury <term>Grim: Often used in seventeenth-century spiritual texts to describe the "dark" or "wrathful" aspect of existence or God’s judgment.</term> and is herself a pure fury, and has enclosed herself in the midst of it through her false desire, which she has awakened within herself. And because God's light does not shine in her, and His love does not touch her, she is a great darkness and a painful, anxious well of fire <note>original: "Vuur-wel," suggesting a source, pit, or bubbling spring of fire.</note>, and carries hell within herself, and cannot see that light of God. Thus she dwells within herself in hell, and there is no need to "enter" it, for wherever she may ever be, she is in

<summary>This page explains the spiritual consequences for a soul that rejects divine surrender, describing how its own sinful desires transform it into a self-contained hell of darkness and fire after death.</summary>
<keywords>afterlife, godless soul, resignation, gelaatenheid, divine wrath, spiritual fire, internal hell, vanity</keywords>

# THE SECOND PART.

<note>original: "G 2" - This is a signature mark used by early printers to help binders assemble the book in the correct order.</note>

...of hell; and even if she could move many hundreds of thousands of miles from her place, she remains in such a boiling and darkness.

The external reason <term>Vernuft: Refers to the human intellect or "wit" that relies only on sensory logic rather than spiritual revelation.</term> asks: How does it happen then that the godless <term>Godlooze: Literally "the godless one," referring to a person who lives without regard for divine will.</term> do not feel hell in this time, since it is so near—yes, even within the person themselves?

Answer: He feels it well in his false conscience, yet he does not understand it, for he still possesses the earthly vanity with which he delights himself, and in which he finds joy and pleasure <term>Wellust: In this 17th-century context, "wellust" refers to any intense sensory pleasure or worldly desire, not exclusively sexual lust.</term>. He also still possesses that external life and that light of nature in which the Soul delights, so that the torment cannot yet be made manifest. 

But when the body dies, the Soul can no longer enjoy such temporal pleasure, and she has also lost that light of the external world. Then she stands in eternal thirst and hunger for such vanity with which she entertained herself here; and yet she can reach nothing but such a false, self-contained will, of which she had far too much in this life, and which yet does not let her be satisfied—of that, she then has too little. Therefore she is in eternal hunger and thirst for vanity, malice, and frivolousness. She would always gladly commit even more malice, yet has nothing in which or with which she can accomplish it; thus such accomplishment happens only within herself, and such a hellish hunger and thirst cannot become fully manifest until the body dies away from her—the body with which she consorted <term>Boeleert: To dally, consort, or behave lewdly; here it suggests a misplaced intimacy with physical pleasures.</term> in pleasure, and which provided her with whatever she lusted after.

Upon death, these distractions vanish, leaving the soul in an eternal, unsatisfied hunger for the sins it once enjoyed.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of the soul’s condition. # JESUS AND THE SOUL.

*The Soul presents to her neighbor her restless wandering, and how she might come to true rest.*

How the Soul struggles <note>original: "topt" — to toil or labor restlessly, like a spinning top.</note>, and seeks with diligence to rest,
Now here, now there, in various delights,
In money, in goods, in luxury and in splendor,
In honor, in fame, in dominion and power,
In lust of the flesh, in drinking and in eating,
In cunning, in art <note>Here "art" refers to human skill or technical craft.</note>, in wisdom and in knowing;
But all in vain, she finds her rest not,
As one clearly sees in both the small and the great.
Thus wanders a stone, lifted from its place,
And driven upward with force by a hand,
It finds no rest until it lies upon the earth,
Its own ground, therefore it falls downward. <note>The author employs a common 17th-century metaphor: just as a stone "wants" to return to its natural place on the ground, the soul is naturally weighted toward God and will remain restless until it "falls" back to Him.</note>
O poor Soul, so far from home and inheritance,
See here an image of your miserable wandering,
If you fell with force upon God, your first ground,
It would be done, you would find your rest immediately;
If you do not do that, then your eternal life must,
O poor Soul, hover in eternal unrest;
That is a fruit too bitter and too tart,
Consider well, eternity lasts so long.

### Divine Answer.

They shall rest on their beds, each one who
<insert>[in]</insert> his integrity has walked.
*The godless, says my God, have no peace.*
Isaiah 57: 2, 21.

Let us then be diligent to enter into that rest: so that no one may fall into that same example of unbelief. Heb. IV: 11. <note>original: "benaarstigen" — to strive with industry or diligence.</note>

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

*The Soul presents to her neighbor her restless wandering, and how she might come to true rest.*

How the Soul struggles <note>original: "topt" — to toil or labor restlessly, like a spinning top.</note>, and seeks with diligence to rest,
Now here, now there, in various delights,
In money, in goods, in luxury and in splendor,
In honor, in fame, in dominion and power,
In lust of the flesh, in drinking and in eating,
In cunning, in skill <note>original: "kunst" — here referring to human ingenuity or technical craft.</note>, in wisdom and in knowing;
But all in vain, she finds her rest not,
As one clearly sees in both the small and the great.
Thus wanders a stone, lifted from its place,
And driven upward with force by a hand,
It finds no rest until it lies upon the earth,
Its own ground, therefore it falls downward. <note>The author employs a common 17th-century metaphor: just as a stone "wants" to return to its natural place on the ground, the soul is naturally weighted toward God and will remain restless until it "falls" back to Him.</note>
O poor Soul, so far from home and inheritance,
See here an image of your miserable wandering,
If you fell with force upon God, your first ground,
It would be done, you would find your rest immediately;
If you do not do that, then your eternal life must,
O poor Soul, hover in eternal unrest;
That is a fruit too bitter and too tart,
Consider well, eternity lasts so long.

### Divine Answer.

They shall rest on their beds, each one who <insert>in</insert> his integrity has walked. *The godless, says my God, have no peace.*
Isaiah 57: 2, 21.
