JESUS AND THE SOUL.

## On the 23rd Emblem. <note>original: "Zinnebeeld" — a symbolic or allegorical image common in 17th-century Dutch devotional books.</note>

### *Of the only resting place of the Soul,*
### *and what it is.*

No thing has rest until it is where it belongs, that is, in its first origin. Throw a stone upward, and it will fall back to the earth; it will not rest until it lies upon the earth again, because the earth is its substance <term>substance: the underlying essence or "stuff" from which a thing is made</term> and its origin. Our body, since it is also an earthly substance, shall likewise not rest until it is in the earth again. But our noble Soul, which is a Spirit, created by God in the image and likeness of God, and which had an eternal being in God, is always restless and comfortless until it rests in God and is united with God.

That filthy earthly flesh may deceive her somewhat, lead her astray, and pull her down toward the earth with it, and for a time sustain her with temporary pleasures and carnal delights; but when she returns to herself again, and looks into her ground <note>original: "grond" — in Dutch mystical tradition, this refers to the deepest, most essential part of the soul where it meets the Divine.</note>, then she sighs again like an exile who is cast out of his country, and like a child who has lost his Father: For the Soul is created by God so noble, subtle, spiritual, and divine, that no thing can satisfy her and make her rest in peace but God Himself; for if she does not rest in God, she is always unsatisfied and restless.

<meta>catchword: So</meta>

<summary>This page explains the theological principle that everything seeks its own origin to find rest, comparing a stone's return to earth with the soul's necessary return to God. It characterizes the soul as a divine spirit that can only be temporarily distracted by earthly pleasures before inevitably longing for its creator.</summary>

<keywords>soul, rest, origin, substance, image of God, restlessness, divine nature, spiritual ground, carnal delights, exile</keywords>

# THE SECOND PART.

So God now calls so kindly to the noble Soul, who (like a dove) has flown over all things and found rest nowhere, to Himself, saying: *Come unto me, all ye that labor and are heavy laden, and I will give you rest.* <note>original: "Komt tot my alle die belast en belaaden zyt, ik zal u verkwikken." — A quotation of Matthew 11:28.</note>
   Come to God; in all things you have sought rest and found none. Come to God, in Him you shall find blessed rest, for He is your God, your origin; He is the eternal rest of the blessed spirits. You have an eternal uncreated being <term>uncreated being: the mystical concept that the soul’s essence exists eternally within God’s mind before its physical creation</term> in Him, and a created being from Him; therefore all creatures <note>original: "schepfelen" — all created things, including animals, plants, and inanimate objects.</note> that were created with you in time by God, point you away from themselves, saying: We are, along with you, created beings of one God and Creator; we have no rest in ourselves, how then should you find rest in us? Therefore go to your origin, from whence your spirit came, and in Him you shall find eternal and blessed rest.

Oh! had I but the wings of a Dove,
That I might fly away from the earth;
I would fly so far until I found rest,
In my God, my true Fatherland.
Fly on, O Soul, fly on, you have both:
Understanding to distinguish good and evil;
Attentiveness, to grasp the distinguished good,
With power and earnestness in the heart; <note>original: "gemood" — literally 'mind' or 'disposition,' but often referring to the seat of emotions and will.</note>
Fly over the flesh and its perverse lusts,
So shall you rest softly in God your Father.

# JESUS AND THE soul.

### *The Soul in contemplation of the work of redemption.*

You saw the wrath of God <term>wrath of God: in 17th-century Dutch theology, this refers to divine justice provoked by sin, often depicted as a consuming fire that only Christ's sacrifice can extinguish</term> in eternal nature,
Kindled like a fire through disobedience.
The Devil, like a Wolf lusting for the blood of sheep,
Firmly gnashing; eternal death and the eternal abyss
Gape open.
On the other side—oh Lord! that was the greatest need—
You saw us weaponless, stripped of all strength,
There was neither help nor counsel; then You had pity
On the fallen Soul, and placed Yourself between them;
You became our shield, and stood so firm and faithful,
The arrows fell sharply and terribly upon You!
This is witnessed by the precious blood, from all Your red wounds,
That flowed upon the earth for all the world's sins;
That was called love indeed, O Soul! where is your reward
For your redemption, that beautiful crown of life?
Ah! who am I, my God, and who are You, my Lord?
With what shall a worm in the dust <note>original: "Worm in 't stof" — a traditional expression of humility, emphasizing the vast distance between the mortal sinner and the Divine King.</note> honor a King?
If You did not accept the crushed heart for Your suffering,
O deepest love of God, then I could not exist,
Yet I remain nonetheless eternally indebted to You,
For the poured-out salvation, so great and manifold.

## Divine Answer.

> And fear not: I am the First and the Last:
> And He that liveth, and I was dead; and behold, I am alive for evermore. Amen; and I have the keys of hell and of death. Revelation 1:17, 18.

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

## On the XXIV. Emblem.

### *A dialogue between the Redeemer and the redeemed Soul.*

**The redeemed Soul speaks:** Ah, my noble <term>pearl: a common mystical symbol for the "hidden treasure" of the divine spark or the Kingdom of Heaven within the human soul</term> pearl, and opened flame of my light within my anxious <term>fire-life: in the mystical tradition of Jakob Böhme, the soul is described as a "fire" that can either burn with the "dark fire" of self-will and anguish or be enlightened into a "light-fire" by divine love</term> fire-life, how you transform me into your joy! O most beautiful Beloved, I became unfaithful to you in my father Adam, and through the power of the fire, I turned myself toward the lust and vanity of the external world and took up an <note>original: "vreemde boelschap," literally meaning a "strange" or "illicit love-affair," here referring to spiritual adultery or seeking satisfaction in worldly things rather than God</note> illicit love; and so I would have had to walk forever in a dark valley of strange dalliance, had you not, in great faithfulness, come to me in this house of my misery—penetrating and breaking through the wrath of God, and of hell, and of dark death—and brought your gentleness and love back into my fire-life.

O Sweet love, you have brought me water of eternal life from God’s spring, and refreshed me in my great thirst: in you I see God’s mercy, which previously stood hidden from me in that illicit love; in you I can rejoice, for you change my fire-anguish into great joy. Ah! most friendly Love, do give me your pearl, so that I may stand in such joy forever.

**The Redeemer Jesus Christ speaks:** My dear Soul, and faithful treasure, you delight me greatly in your beginning; it is true, I have broken through the deep doors of God to reach you, through God’s wrath, through hell and death, into that house of your miseries, and have granted you my love out of grace, and delivered you from the chains and bonds to which you were tied fast; I have kept my faithfulness to you, but you currently desire

<summary>This page presents a mystical dialogue (Emblem 24) where the Soul thanks Christ for rescuing it from "spiritual adultery" and the "fire-life" of worldly vanity, transitioning from anguish to joy through divine mercy.</summary>
<keywords>Redemption, Fire-life, Spiritual Adultery, Pearl of Great Price, Divine Love, Adam, Wrath of God, Dialogue</keywords>

--- Page 127 ---
<meta>This page continues a dialogue between a divine voice and the soul, focusing on the preservation of the "pearl" of faith or divine spark against the dangers of the material and spiritual worlds.</meta>

# THE SECOND PART.

...it is a grave matter for me, and one I would not gladly venture with you. You wish to have my pearl as your own property; remember, my dear Soul, how you neglected it previously in Adam <note>The author uses "Adam" here to represent the fallen state of humanity, through which the original divine connection was lost.</note>, and moreover, you still stand in great danger, and walk in two perilous realms: as with your deepest root, the <term>fire-origin<note>original: "vuur-oorſprong." A term used in mystical philosophy (likely Jacob Böhme) to describe the raw, energetic power of God’s nature which can be experienced as a "consuming fire" or wrath if not balanced by divine light.</note></term>, you walk in the land where God calls himself a strong, jealous God, and a consuming fire.

In the other realm you walk in the external world; in the air, in purely corrupted flesh and blood, where the world's pleasure, along with the Devil's assaults, rushes over you every hour: you might, in your great joy, introduce earthliness into my beauty again, and darken my little pearl for me. Also, you might become haughty, just as Lucifer became when he held the pearl as a possession, and you might turn away from God's harmony; then I would hereafter be eternally deprived of my fellowship.

I wish to keep my pearl within me, and I wish to dwell in heaven within you—within your faded inner humanity, which I have now made alive again—and preserve my pearl for Paradise, until you cast off this earthliness: then I will give you my pearl as a possession. But my countenance, and the sweet rays of the pearl, I will gladly offer to you during the time of this earthly life.

<summary>In this passage, a divine voice explains to the soul why the "pearl" of spiritual grace cannot yet be fully possessed as personal property, warning of the dangers posed by the soul's fallen nature and the temptations of the physical world.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, spiritual pearl, fire-origin, Adam, Lucifer, inner humanity, divine fellowship, mystical theology</keywords>

I saw the world broad and wide,
Overspread with a thousand deceits;
That might rightly make a heart tremble,
Countless were all the snares;
The cast net <note>The "cast net" (geſchooten net) is a classic emblem for the sudden and inescapable temptations of the world.</note> stood everywhere,
The hook for the feet lay in the valley,
And very hidden along the paths;
A deceit risen from the abyss,
Had quite deceptively here and there,
Many treacherous bridges, full of danger,
Built with craft—oh, craft of hell!
So keen to set evil to work!
I sighed and spoke: "Alas, Father! Alas!
My God, my fairest Love, who may—
Who may escape that multitude of dangers,
Those snares and that treacherous yarn?" <note>original: "looze garen." This refers to the fine, almost invisible threads used to weave a hunter's snare, representing subtle sins.</note>
Then it was clearly said to me:

JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### On the 25th Emblem.

->*Concerning the manifold snares of the Devil, and how the same are to be escaped.*<-

The Devil is a master of a thousand arts; the wide world is full of his cunning attacks, deceitful snares, and nets. He lurks after the Soul like a skillful bird-catcher; he seeks to plunge coarse sinners deeper and deeper into sin, and by means of worldly things, to keep them away from heavenly ones. Those who are already on the good path, he seeks to lead astray again; and in those who have already come far, he seeks to stir up selfhood <note>original: "eigenheid." This refers to the ego or the "own-self" as opposed to being centered in God.</note>, in order to exalt them in spiritual pride. Indeed, he can even present himself as an Angel of Light <note>A reference to 2 Corinthians 11:14, warning that spiritual deception can appear holy.</note>, so that even a practiced Soul has enough to do to distinguish whether that which sometimes appears to it be Divine or Devilish.

What then is the safest way to escape these dangers?

None other than the simple way of true humility.

The will of man, with all its ingenuity and desire, shall sink entirely into itself like an unworthy child that is not worthy of the high grace of God. It must attribute to itself neither knowledge nor understanding, nor pray for or desire any understanding of God within the creaturely selfhood; but place itself plainly and simply in the love and grace

<meta>catchword: grace</meta>

<summary>A prose meditation on the 25th emblem, describing the Devil as a "master of a thousand arts" who uses spiritual pride to trap the Soul. It concludes that the only safe passage through these snares is the path of total humility and "sinking" into the grace of God.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual warfare, snares of the devil, humility, Angel of Light, selfhood, spiritual pride, bird-catcher</keywords>

--- Page 131 ---
<meta>This page continues the spiritual instruction on how to escape the Devil's snares through complete surrender. It follows the previous page's argument that man must abandon his own cleverness and sink into the "grace of God."</meta>

# THE SECOND PART.

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

of God, sink into Christ Jesus, and desire his intellect <term>vernuft: human cleverness or ingenuity</term> and selfhood <term>zelfheid: the ego-driven nature of the individual</term> to be as if dead in the life of God, and entirely surrender himself to the life of God in love, so that He may do with him as with His instrument, however and whatever He wills. That self-intellect shall not undertake any fabrications <note>original: "dichten." In this context, it refers to intellectual concoctions or human speculations about divine matters.</note> in divine things or human foundations, nor will or desire anything except God's grace in Christ alone. Just as a child constantly longs for its mother's breast, so shall the hunger [of the soul] steadily enter into God's love, and not allow itself to be pulled away from such hunger. 

When that outward intellect triumphs in the light and speaks: "I possess that true child" <note>This refers to the "New Birth" or the spiritual child born within the soul in mystical tradition.</note>, then the will of the desire shall bow to the earth and, in that highest humility, introduce a humble unknowing, and say to it: "you are foolish; you must wrap yourself in that same humility with great humbleness, and become entirely nothing in yourself. You must neither know nor love yourself; everything that is on and in you must consider and hold itself as nothing, but merely as an instrument of God." It must lead that desire only into God's mercy, and go out from all self-knowing and self-willing, holding it all as nothing, and creating no will to ever enter back into it, whether near or far.

And when this happens, the natural will enters into its own powerlessness, and the Devil no longer has any power to sift <note>A reference to Luke 22:31, where Satan desires to "sift" the disciples like wheat.</note> him with his false desires, for those places of his rest <note>The "places of rest" for the Devil are the human self-will and pride; without them, he has no foothold.</note> become entirely parched and powerless for him.

<vocab><term>Christus</term> (Christ), <term>Jezus</term> (Jesus), <term>ootmoedigheid</term> (humility), <term>vernuft</term> (intellect/ingenuity), <term>zelfheid</term> (selfhood/ego), <term>Duivel</term> (Devil), <term>erbarmen</term> (mercy), <term>natuurlyke wille</term> (natural will)</vocab>

<summary>This page explains that the only way to escape the Devil's influence is to surrender the human intellect and ego entirely to God, becoming a "nothing" or a mere instrument of His will. By embracing a state of humble "unknowing" and spiritual hunger, the soul deprives the Devil of the prideful foothold he needs to manipulate the human will.</summary>

<keywords>surrender, humility, selfhood, intellect, instrument of God, spiritual hunger, New Birth, spiritual warfare, natural will</keywords>

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

->*The Soul resigned <term>gelaaten: a state of total surrender or "Gelassenheit," where the soul lets go of all personal desire</term> and still in the fire of purification.*<-

No, sweetest JESUS, source of love and kindliness,
You take no pleasure in the poor Soul's distress,
No fire proceeds from You; the flames of this smart <note>original: "smerte" - meaning intense physical or emotional pain</note>
Spring from the depths of the unpurified heart,
My own inequality and the flaws of nature,
Are themselves the firewood of this sharp, consuming fire,
And I neither wish nor want to be released or unbound,
Until this living fire has devoured its food;
Whether it lasts long or short, we comfort ourselves in the pain,
For otherwise I might never be united with God;
Let it burn as it will, yes, let the flames grow,
Until the self-will is entirely consumed to ash,
Then the fire shall quench itself like a life that dies,
Which, consumed and worn away, lacks its nurturing food.
O Self-will <term>Eigen wil: the ego-driven desire that separates the individual from the Divine</term>, you who have so long separated me
From God, the highest good, how I shall rejoice
In your destruction; for behold, after your death
I am in God, my Love, delivered from all need.
Ah! sweetest JESUS, ah! strengthen me with Your power,
As long as this suffering lasts, so that I do not faint.

## On the XXVI. Emblem.

### *Of the purification of the Soul through suffering.*

Once a person has been purified, there is yet a further purification, and then one even further still. This dying and perishing <note>original: "verworden" — referring to the decomposition of the old self</note> increases, just as that growth increases.

No one should think that he who is assured of eternal life does not have to suffer because of it.

When the chosen ones enter into hell <note>In this mystical context, "hell" refers to a state of intense spiritual purgation or the "dark night of the soul" rather than eternal punishment.</note>, they suffer it gladly out of love, even though it is a great pain to them; just as someone who gives themselves over to suffering for another whom they heartily love does not desire to be released from the suffering—even though it is a great trial to them—so that the other might be free from their suffering.

The greater the death, hell, and damnation, the greater the life, heaven, and bliss. The greater the death, hell, and anguish, the greater and higher the birth, heaven, and life that follows afterward.

Christ speaks: *Whoever does not forsake [all that he has, he cannot be my disciple], etc.* <note>original: "Wie niet en verlaat, enz."</note> Luke XIV: 33. That which a person is to forsake, he must first possess. Thus, a person first has Father, Mother, Friends, riches, worldly honor, skill <note>original: "konst" — can also mean human artifice or worldly learning</note>, etc. and

<meta>catchword: these</meta>

<summary>This page explains the mystical process of spiritual refining, arguing that suffering and a "spiritual death" are necessary precursors to divine rebirth. It uses the paradox that the deeper one sinks into the "hell" of self-surrender, the higher they rise into heavenly life.</summary>
<keywords>purification, suffering, spiritual death, rebirth, Luke 14:33, self-surrender, divine love, Jan Luyken</keywords>

--- Page 135 ---
<meta>This page continues the prose commentary on the 26th Emblem, following the discussion of why the "chosen ones" must experience spiritual suffering. It shifts the focus to the iterative nature of spiritual growth, where each level of attainment requires a new "death" or relinquishing of the previous state to reach an even higher degree of purity. The author uses the Apostle Paul as a primary example of this ongoing spiritual labor.</meta>

Man is hidden <note>This implies that a person’s true spiritual state is often concealed from their own self-awareness.</note> from his own sight, possessed by many things of which he is unaware, which he only discovers when they are taken away from him. Man also sometimes still feels a sense of decline <note>original: "afgangs" — this refers to a spiritual waning, a loss of inner strength or the "ebbing" of divine comfort.</note> within himself and does not know what it is; this at times falls so heavily upon him that the Lord must moderate <note>original: "maatigen" — to temper or soften the intensity of the trial so the soul is not overwhelmed.</note> it.

That we enter into such great suffering is not because God takes pleasure in our suffering, but because He removes from us those things upon which we rest <note>Meaning the earthly comforts or certainties that we lean on instead of God.</note>, and it is this removal that causes us such suffering.

->*The Soul clinging fast to her God.*<-

Oh no! I will not leave you, oh no!
You cannot, nor shall you, flee from me;
I hold to God, my only ONE <note>The capitalization of "EEN" (One) emphasizes the Neoplatonic and mystical idea of God as the Absolute Unity, the source of all being.</note>,
Let it go as it will; and even if it happened
That earth and the kingdom of heaven perished,
It does not affect me; let that happen.
For the sake of One, I leave all things;
Who would tear that away from my **WILL**?
My God, my Love, my only good,
Even if my body and soul went to ruin,
As long as there is still within my spirit,
A gasp <note>original: "snak" — a desperate breath or yearning sigh.</note>, a little spark to be found,
So long shall you be loved by me;
With sweetness you have captured me,
Just as one lures a playing child;
Now I delight in clinging to you.
You are my ground <note>original: "grond" — the "ground" is a mystical term for the innermost essence of a person where the soul and God are united.</note>; where would I go?
You are my God; I hold you quietly.
My **WILL-SPIRIT** <note>original: "Wille-geest" — this refers to the higher faculty of the human soul that has been purified and directed entirely toward God's will.</note> grasps at nothing else;
You may do with me according to your Will.

JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### Upon the XXVII. Emblem.

->*Of a strong clinging of the Soul to God, in true resignation <term>resignation: the original Dutch "gelaatenheid" refers to a state of total surrender or "letting go" of one's own will to be filled by God.</term>.*<-

Though it seemed that you would leave me, O God my Father! and that you, hiding your face, spoke to me: I do not want you; yet I want you, and I want nothing other than you. If I may not have you, then I want nothing. Now that I have once come to love you, all things that are not you are vain to me, and they cannot satisfy me. You cannot flee from me, O God! my Beloved; for I hang upon your neck with my will. I have turned my will away from everything to hold fast to you alone; I have surrendered my will entirely into your will. Therefore, O Lord! do with me as it is most pleasing to you. I surrender and offer myself into the hidden abyss <note>original: "afgrond" — a term frequently used by mystics to describe the bottomless, infinite nature of God's being.</note> of your Divinity, O Lord! whether you wish to preserve me or damn me, that stands in your power; your will be done to me.

Even if my body and Soul (if it were possible) should perish, as long as there is still a spark of life in me, so long will I love you, so long will I hold to you, and otherwise I will grasp at nothing with my will.

With the sweetness of wounded love <note>A reference to "Amor Vulneratus," a common motif where the Soul is "wounded" by divine love, making it yearn only for the Creator.</note> you have drawn me to yourself, and brought me to the captive love where I could not run away from you, and now I am

It emphasizes that once the Soul has tasted divine love, all worldly things become empty and meaningless.</summary>

# THE THIRD PART.

...fallen into this raging love <note>original: "raazende liefde" — an ecstatic, intense divine love that overwhelms the senses and reason</note>, let everything go now as it will, I shall never leave you more.

Ah! that we may thus speak without lying, from an upright heart; to that end may the flowing fountain of eternal goodness help us. Amen.

A person should keep himself in suffering without the amusement of creatures <note>meaning: seeking comfort in earthly things or other people</note>, and seek neither comforter nor sympathizer, but leave all that to the Lord: let him be unconsoled and unpitied until the Lord comes, and let him despise every strange outburst and evasion, and await the Lord. The Lord will hold faithfully to him in right measure and order, letting him suffer for His honor, and for the person's progress or spiritual bringing-forth: which the person would hinder if, being un-resigned <term>un-resigned: "ongelaaten," describing a state where the soul is restless and resists God's will by seeking its own relief</term> in suffering, he did not await the Lord.

That suffering carries a great fruit within itself; therefore one must wait upon the Lord therein.

The Psalmist speaks, *I have waited long for the Lord; and he has inclined himself to me, and heard my cry.* Psalm 40:2.

*I wait for the Lord, from one morning to the next.* Psalm 130:6.

And Isaiah: *O, well is it with all those who wait upon God! those who expect him shall never be put to shame.* Isaiah 30:18.

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

*The Soul refreshed by Divine grace.*

So is a Rose-garden bathed with morning dew,
->refreshed,<-
And blown upon softly and kindly by the West wind!
Just as the Lord’s spirit cools my face,
Now I, despite all the enemy’s raging,
Made green in Paradise, through a sharp death <note>referring to the "death" of the ego or worldly desires necessary for spiritual rebirth</note>,
Am my Lord’s little flower, sprinkled with his blood,
Which he poured upon my leaves out of pure love.
That is a dew of life that can nourish the spiritual body.
His power is my garden, his friendly face
My beautiful sunshine; O world! delay no longer,
Pass through the death of your self into the uncreated light <term>uncreated light: "ongeschapen licht," a mystical concept referring to the eternal, divine radiance of God that existed before physical light was created</term>,
In dying, your ground becomes pregnant with the true life,
And bears the new child, which, begotten by God’s spirit,
Beautifully resembles its origin, and with a full abundance
Plays for eternity before the eyes of God its Father,
A blessedness that neither I nor anyone can describe;
O worldly wisdom, foolish wit, how blind you are,
And think that no one, though he breaks through hell and
->sins,<-
Finds the beautiful Paradise in this life:
No, it is never found by the old animal <note>original: "oude dier" — a reference to the unregenerate, carnal nature of man before spiritual transformation</note>,
But the newborn child, of water and of Spirit,
Is led by the Bridegroom <note>A traditional mystical title for Jesus, representing his union with the Soul</note> into great joys:
And sometimes beholds that land, where it fears no
->departure,<-
But shall dwell eternally, clothed in beautiful virtues.

### *The Soul refreshed by Divine grace.*

Thus a rose garden is washed with morning dew,
->blown upon by the West wind, soft and friendly!<-
Just as the Lord's spirit cools my countenance,
Now, despite all the enemy’s raging,
Rooted in Paradise, through a sharp death,
I am my Lord's little flower, sprinkled with His blood,
Which He, out of pure love, poured upon my leaves.
That is a dew of life that can nourish the spiritual body.
His power is my garden, His friendly countenance
My beautiful sunshine; O world! tarry no longer,
Go through the death of yourself <note>The "death of self" (Dutch: "dood uws zelfs") refers to the mystical process of "Gelassenheid" or letting go of one's ego and desires to make room for God.</note> into the uncreated light,
In dying, your ground becomes pregnant with the true life,
And bears the new child, who, begotten by God's spirit,
Beautifully resembles its origin, and with full abundance
Plays for eternity before the eyes of God its Father,
A blessedness, that neither I nor anyone can depict;
O world's wisdom, foolish wit, how blind you are,
To believe that no one—though he breaks through hell and
->sins—<-
Finds the beautiful Paradise in this life:
No, it is never found by the old beast <note>original: "oude dier" — a reference to the "old man" or the carnal, unregenerated nature of humanity mentioned in Pauline theology.</note>,
But the newborn child, of water and of Spirit,
Is led by the Bridegroom into great joys:
And sometimes beholds that land, where it fears no
->departure,<-
But shall dwell eternally, clothed in beautiful virtues.

### Upon the XXVIII. Emblem.
<note>In 17th-century literature, an 'emblem' (zinnebeeld) combined an illustration, a motto, and a poem or prose to teach a moral or spiritual lesson.</note>

->*Of thanks and praise from the Soul, to her Redeemer.*<-

Now to you, O great God, in your power and sweetness, be praise, thanks, strength, honor, and glory, that you have delivered me from the driver of anguish: <note>The 'driver of anguish' (dryver der angst) likely refers to the devil or the torment of a guilty conscience that pursues the soul.</note> O you most beautiful Love, my heart embraces you; where have you been for so long? I thought I was in Hell, in the wrath of God. O most friendly Love, do stay with me, be my joy and refreshment, guide me on straight paths: into your love I surrender myself. Ah! I am dark before you, do make me light, O noble Love, give me your sweet pearl, <note>The 'pearl' is a common mystical metaphor for the kingdom of God or the indwelling of Christ, based on the 'pearl of great price' from the Gospel of Matthew.</note> do place it within me.

O great God in Christ Jesus, now I praise and laud you in your truth, great power, and glory, that you have forgiven my sins and filled me with your power: I rejoice in you in my life, and praise you in your firmament, which no one can enclose except your spirit, in your mercy: my bones rejoice in your power, and my heart plays in your love. Thanks be to you eternally, that you have delivered me from hell and turned death within me into life: presently I find your promised truth, O sweetest Love! do not let me depart from you again, do honor me with your crown of pearls, and remain in me: be my own, that I may rejoice in you eternally.

->The Soul speaks of the pure union with God.<-

Just like a drop of water, poured into red wine,
Loses itself entirely in taste, in smell, and color;
So purely must the spirit be melted into God,
And die completely, to the very depths of her own will; <note>The "dying of the self-will" is a central theme in 17th-century mysticism, representing the surrender of personal desire to follow God's lead.</note>
That is the demand that God makes of his creatures,
All that exalts itself and resists here will be spat out;
Love does this from a sincere and pure mind,
That always lies bowed in humility before its God.
What folly keeps us back from this true death? <note>This "true death" refers to "mystical death," or the dying to the worldly ego so the soul can live in God.</note>
Oh, blessed sinking into a sea of true delight,
Where poor humanity is beautifully and richly nourished <note>original: "vermaat" — an archaic term for being fed or measured out sustenance.</note>
And wears the crown of all that eternal wisdom has formed.
Consider rightly, my Soul, if you find the God of peace,
In the eternal Kingdom, where out of so many minds,
Out of so many thousands, only one WILL proceeds,
In the eternal origin and fountain of all good.
My Love, my Bridegroom, my most beautiful Lord!
Oh, unique eternal ONE, my wealth, my high value,
You are the only all, and all that I desire.
Whom have I beside you in heaven or on earth?

I in them, and you in me. *John 17:21, 23.*

## Upon the XXIX. Emblem.

### *Of the union with God.*

When we mention the word love, we mean by it a uniting power which desires to make one thing out of him who loves and that which is loved. However, since it is not possible for two things to become entirely and in every way one thing, unless one were to pass away entirely; so love nevertheless seeks that closest and most fitting union. And although it is true that parables can instruct man a little, they are nonetheless as unlike the true union—which the Soul may have with God—as God is higher than all creatures.

Consider the grafted tree: the graft, through the nourishment of the earth, becomes one tree with the trunk; so the Soul, through the nourishment of grace and love, becomes one spirit with the Spirit of God.

When one lets a drop of water fall into a vessel of wine, that water changes into wine; it loses its own nature and takes on the nature of the Wine, in color, smell, taste, and all properties. Likewise, the Soul also falls into that immensity of God, like a drop of water into the greatness of the Sea, and it retains only the essence of the Soul, but all its powers are <term>deified<note>The original Dutch "vergood" refers to the mystical process of *theosis*, where the human soul is permeated by divine nature without losing its distinct identity.</note></term>, that is, permeated by God, like a

<meta>catchword: star,</meta>

<summary>This page explains the nature of the mystical union, using the metaphors of a grafted tree and a drop of water in wine to describe how the soul is transformed by divine love and grace.</summary>
<keywords>mystical union, love, grace, deification, metaphors, grafting, water and wine, Jan Luyken</keywords>

--- Page 151 ---
<meta>Page 129 continues the explanation of the mystical union between the Soul and God, picking up from the metaphor of the star mentioned at the end of page 128.</meta>

<page-num>129</page-num>
<header>THE THIRD PART.</header>

star, which being dark by nature, is flooded through with the clarity of the sun.

There is yet another parable of two mirrors: when they are placed opposite one another, each receives the image of the other entirely within itself, along with its own image that is reflected in the other mirror. So it is also with eternity and the human heart; when these words from the book of Love <note>The "book of Love" refers to the Song of Solomon (Song of Songs), a biblical book frequently used by mystics to describe the relationship between the Soul and God.</note> are fulfilled: *My Beloved is turned toward me, and I am turned toward him*; then these two mirrors are set against each other. When God then wishes to illuminate the Soul with the light of Majesty, the Soul receives that image and the clarity, and the knowing and <term>experiencing<note>The original Dutch *gebruiken* (to use) in this mystical context refers to the "fruitio Dei" or the joyful enjoyment and experience of God's presence.</note></term> of God within itself perfectly, and its own knowing and experiencing in God, much more perfectly than those outward mirrors do. For those always remain essentially separated from each other; but the Soul, as soon as it receives that glorious image of the eternal mirror in its incomprehensible clarity, is in that same moment united with that incomprehensible, glorious, clear divine mirror, and therein swallowed up and melted away, just as a drop of water melts into wine, or like a flying spark in a great fire.

### The Soul against the World.

Yes, it is all the same; however foolish I may be in your eyes,
Just as foolish, O world! you are in turn to me.
As foolish as you judge me, because I hate <term>pleasure<note>original: *wellust*; in this context, it refers to the fleeting sensory pleasures and carnal desires of the world rather than the joy found in God.</note></term>,
And leave all things for the sake of the only Good;
So foolish I deem you again, because you keep everything
For the only true Good, and choose the mire over the gold.
"Yes," you say, "yours is but wind; I touch my treasure with my hands."
O foolish creature! hide yourself now for shame,
Are you so wise, and yet you do not let a beautiful diamond—
Though you dismiss it as wind—slip from your
->hand;<-
Much less shall you rob me—though you call it smoke and winds—
Of the <note>The "pearl" refers to the "Pearl of Great Price" from the Gospel of Matthew, representing the Kingdom of Heaven or the indwelling of Christ.</note>pearl that I find within my heart.
O foolish creature! the living mind
Often grasps more firmly than you ever do with hands;
But it is in vain to struggle with you, O <term>blind intellect<note>original: *blind vernuft*; refers to human reasoning that lacks the light of faith or spiritual insight.</note></term>!
As day and night differ, so are we also divided;
I am a fruit in the light, you are in the darkness,
Each grows from its own soil until the time of ripening,
Then each shall harvest their fruit with joy in their barns.
Your treasure falls away from you; my treasure shall endure forever.

---

Matthew VI: 19, 20, 21.

## On the XXX. Emblem.

### *Concerning the treasure of the soul, and the coarseness of worldly people.*

There is no greater folly to the world than the life of a Christian; for it sees him cast away and despise as dung all those things in which it places its salvation and highest pleasure; and because the world is coarse, blunt, and animalistic, it does not perceive why this abandonment occurs; it calls him a fool and insane, seeing that he sells all his goods; but of the high-worthy treasure, more precious than all the kingdoms of the earth, which he has found in the <term>Field<note>A reference to the Parable of the Hidden Treasure in Matthew 13:44: "The kingdom of heaven is like treasure hidden in a field. When a man found it, he hid it again, and then in his joy went and sold all he had and bought that field."</note></term>, it knows nothing. It believes that he satisfies himself with smoke and wind, for it knows no good nor wealth other than what it touches with its animalistic hands; whatever one says to it of the touching or feeling of the <term>soul<note>original: *gemoed*; this term refers to the inner mind, heart, and spiritual disposition—the seat of human consciousness and emotion.</note></term>, it cannot understand, believing it to be imagination and deceptive dreams; nevertheless, the world ought not to be so dull as to be unable to understand that the noblest and most subtle part of man is less likely to be deceived than the coarse and the blunt. Now indeed, the soul of man is the fountain and wellspring of life, more so than the external members of the coarse body, which receive their movement from the soul's outgoing powers, and more so than the external senses, which arise from the soul as if from their root; do you think now that this <term>noble ground<note>original: *edele grond*; in Rhenish-Flemish mysticism, the "ground" of the soul is the deepest, most essential part of a human being where the divine and human meet.</note></term> cannot distinguish whether it possesses essential truth or a fleeting vapor, while you in your coarseness well know how to distinguish whether you have a pearl or a straw in your external hand? O blinded human, how have you been so entirely given over to the animalistic life! Oh, how foolish and insane are

[...it is a shame for] you before us, that you choose that which is temporary and perishable over that which is eternal and imperishable. O, to what an unfaithful friend do you bind yourself—to this deceitful world, which brings its lovers not the slightest comfort in the hour of death, abandons them, and lets them sail away poor, naked, and wretched. You see this indeed daily before your eyes; why then are you still so mad? Oh, how gladly we would have you as our companions! In the eternally green and fruit-bearing Paradise, where God our Father expects us so kindly, and desires to embrace us as His dear children of grace in His fatherly arms, where we shall play eternally in full peace and joy before His most holy face.

Oh, do become wise, and do not take it for a delusion; the things that seem far off finally draw near. Wake up, and notice with attention whether you belong in this world.

If you eat the fruits of a foreign land, you surely believe that there is such a land where those fruits have grown; why then should the <term>Soul<note>original: *Ziele*</note></term>, eating the sweet fruits of Paradise, still doubt whether there might also be a Paradise?

O no! And were you wise, you would believe those who would rather die than lie to you; you generally believe the common world well enough—do not let your heart be hardened and blinded any longer by him <note>A reference to the Devil or the spirit of the world, who seeks to keep the soul in his "company" or bondage.</note> who would gladly have you in his company eternally.

## On the 30th Emblem.

### *Of the treasure of the spirit, and of the coarseness of worldly people.*

There is no greater foolishness in the eyes of the world than the life of a Christian; for the world sees him throwing away and despising all those things in which it places its salvation and highest pleasure as if they were <term>filth<note>original: *drek*; a strong term used in 17th-century spiritual literature to denote something worthless or offensive, often echoing the Apostle Paul’s language in Philippians 3:8 regarding worldly gains.</note></term>; and because the world is coarse, blunt, and <term>animalistic<note>original: *diers*; referring to a life lived purely through physical instincts and senses without spiritual awareness.</note></term>, it does not perceive why this abandonment takes place. It calls him foolish and insane, seeing that he sells all his goods; but of the highly precious treasure, more valuable than all the Kingdoms of the earth, which he has found in the <term>Field<note>A reference to the Parable of the Hidden Treasure in Matthew 13:44, where a man sells everything he has to buy a field containing a hidden treasure, representing the Kingdom of Heaven.</note></term>, it knows nothing. The world thinks that he satisfies himself with smoke and wind, for it knows no good nor wealth other than that which it touches with its animal hands; what one tells it regarding the touch or feeling of the <term>spirit<note>original: *gemoed*; in this context, it refers to the inner soul or the seat of consciousness where spiritual truths are felt.</note></term>, it cannot understand, thinking that these are merely imagination and deceptive dreams. Yet, people ought not to be so blunt that they could not understand that the most noble and subtle part of a person is less likely to be deceived than that which is coarse and blunt. For indeed, the spirit of man is the fountain and wellspring of life, more so than the external members of the coarse body, which receive their movement from the spirit's outflowing powers; and more so than the external senses, which arise from the spirit as if from their root. Do you think now that this noble foundation cannot distinguish whether it possesses substantial truth or a fleeting vapor, while you, in your coarseness, well know how to distinguish whether you have a pearl or a straw in your external hand? O Blinded man, how you are so entirely surrendered to the animal life! Oh, how foolish and insane are

<summary>A contemplative prose piece arguing that the "coarse" worldly person cannot understand the spiritual "treasure" found by a Christian, because the world relies only on physical touch while the soul relies on a deeper, more reliable inner perception.</summary>
<keywords>Christian life, spiritual treasure, worldliness, discernment, the soul, Jan Luyken, emblem, animalistic vs. spiritual</keywords>

<header>THE THIRD PART.</header>

...[it seems to] us, that you choose that which is temporary and perishable over that which is eternal and incorruptible. Oh, to what an unfaithful friend do you bind yourself—to this deceptive world, which brings its lovers not the slightest comfort in the hour of death, but abandons them, and lets them drift away poor, naked, and miserable. You see this before your eyes daily; why then are you still so <term>foolish<note>original: *dol*; implies a state of being mad or senseless, common in 17th-century Dutch moral literature to describe those blinded by worldly vanity.</note></term>? Oh, how gladly would we have you as our <term>companions<note>original: *gespelen*; literally "playmates." This reflects the "Little Child" theology of the period, where the soul is seen as a child playing in the garden of God's grace.</note></term>! In the eternally greening and fruit-bearing Paradise, where God our Father awaits us so kindly, and desires to embrace us as his dear children of grace in his fatherly arms, where we shall play eternally in full peace and joy before his most holy face.

Oh, do become wise, and do not take it for a delusion; the things that seem far off come near at last. Wake up, and notice with attention whether you belong at home in this world.

If you eat the fruits of a foreign land, you surely believe that there is such a land where those fruits were grown; why then should the Soul, eating the sweet fruits of Paradise, still doubt whether there might also be a Paradise?

Oh no! And were you wise, you would believe those who would rather die than lie to you; you usually believe the common world well enough—do not let your heart be hardened and blinded any longer by him <note>A reference to the Devil or the Spirit of the Age.</note> who would gladly have you in his company forever.

### *The Soul compares all the world's doings to child's play.*

Are you so amazed, O wise worldling,
That I do not rejoice in any worldly thing?
Just as little as you can truly delight your heart,
Or find real contentment in the smallest matters,
And trifles, with which an innocent child plays,
Who imagines something great from something small and trivial,
So little can all these earthly things give to me,
With which you spend the time of your whole life:
Money and goods, gold and silver, precious stones,
Delicious food and drink, elegant clothes,
Power and prestige, served and worshipped,
Academic learning, art, and worldly customs,
Luxury and lust, and carnal ease,
And other things that only serve to increase the pack—
The coarse and heavy burden of the Soul.
I look upon all that as you look at children's <term>knucklebones<note>original: *kooten*; an ancient game similar to jacks or marbles played with sheep bones. Here it represents the worthlessness of worldly pursuits compared to spiritual ones.</note></term>,
And lesser toys, with which the little boy amuses himself
In his lack of wisdom, before he reaches <term>maturity<note>original: *deftigheid*; while it means "dignity" or "gravity" today, in this context it refers to the state of being a grown, serious adult.</note></term>:
But praise be to God, we have outgrown that childhood;
The pleasure of child's play would not suit us well
Before the Father's eyes, who called us to something higher,
When He created the Soul so beautiful to be His <term>beloved<note>original: *vriendinne*; literally "female friend," but in the mystical tradition of Jan Luyken, it denotes the Soul as the Bride of Christ.</note></term>.
He Himself is the treasure of the Soul, and those who find Him,
Regard all else, with me, as vanity and wind.

### Divine Answer.

*Seek those things which are above, where Christ is sitting at the right hand of God. Set your mind on things above, not on things on the earth. Colossians 3: verses 1, 2.*

I beheld all the works that are done under the Sun: and behold, all was vanity, and vexation of spirit. Ecclesiastes 1: 14.

Ecclesiastes 2: 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 11. I made for myself great works, I built myself houses, I planted myself vineyards.

I made myself gardens, and pleasure-grounds, and I planted trees in them, of all kinds of fruit.

I made myself pools of water, to water therewith the wood that brings forth trees.

I also gathered for myself silver and gold, and the treasures of Kings and of the provinces: I provided myself with male and female Singers, and the delights of the children of men, musical instruments <note>original: *snaarenspel*; literally "string-play," referring to lutes, harps, and other instrumental music</note>, yes, all kinds of musical instruments, etc.

Then I looked on all my works that my hands had made, and on the labor that I had labored to do: and behold, all was vanity and vexation of spirit, and there was no profit in it under the Sun.

Everything that is not God, or does not happen for God’s sake, is vanity. All works and words that are not done for God’s sake and to his glory, are lost. To leave all things to gain God, that is true wisdom, for God is the only good.

### *The Soul praises spiritual beauty high above the worldly.*

We are not so poor as you might think,
That we would love no beauty,
All our adornment is within,
Not borrowed from a worm or cattle. <note>The poet refers to silk, produced by silkworms, and leather or wool from livestock—the raw materials of worldly fashion.</note>
What ought most to be praised?
You deck out a beast that soon vanishes,
We beautifully deck an eternal spirit,
With high colors, from God's essence.
They are spirit-thoughts, if you so consider them,
No dreams nor trifles,
But true, essential things,
Which are tangible, consisting of power.
And if you saw the garment; the body of the Souls,
As thin and clear as crystal,
Flowed through by the eternal sunshine,
You would kneel before this beauty;
We would not trade with any King's wife,
For silk, purple, and scarlet,
And could not make her so beautiful,
Even if she were stiff with gold and pearls.
Therefore, O man! you are deceived,
Because you see no beauty on the outside,
The true beauty you do not know,
A darkness covers your eyes.

### Divine Answer.

The King's daughter is all glorious within. <note>original: *Des Konings dochter is geheel verheerlykt, inwendig*; a reference to the soul's inner sanctity.</note>
Psalm XLV: 14. <note>In many English translations, this is Psalm 45:13.</note>

The text concludes with a scriptural validation that true glory resides within the soul.</summary>

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

## On the XXXII. Emblem.

### *Of the Internal Spiritual Beauty.*

**W**hen one speaks of internal ornaments and beauties, you must not think that these are merely parables: oh no, it is all essential reality. If you saw the beauty of the new birth, you could not marvel at it enough. A diamond, ruby, or other precious stone, lying in the clear sunshine, is very translucent, glittering, and beautiful. The new creature, which grows hidden within the old coarse person through the nourishment of the water of eternal life—which fills the entire Godhead—and through the Holy Spirit, is as translucent as crystal; it compares itself to the eternal light of the divine Majesty, by which it is entirely shone through. What kind of beauty this is cannot be expressed in words, but God can show it to whom He wills by the opening of the spiritual eyes.

When a person, through all exercises, draws the outer person into the inner rational <term>vernuftigen: refers to the 'intellectual' or 'rational' soul, a higher faculty of the mind in 17th-century psychology</term> person, and these two persons (namely the sensory powers and the rational powers) offer themselves entirely in unison into the innermost person—that is, into the mystery of the spirit where that true divine image lies—and then penetrate entirely into the divine abyss <term>goddelyken afgrond: a mystical term describing the unfathomable and infinite depth of God’s essence</term>, where the person was eternally in their uncreated state <term>ongeschapenheid: the mystical concept that the essence of the human soul existed in the mind of God before time began</term>: and when the merciful God finds the person thus in their purity, and turned toward Him in nakedness <note>In mystical literature, "nakedness" refers to a soul stripped of worldly attachments and ego.</note>, the divine fatherly abyss leans toward them, and sinks into the pure, turned ground, and upon that He forms the

<meta>catchword: spi-</meta>

<summary>This page explores the mystical reality of the "new birth," comparing the soul's inner beauty to the brilliance of a diamond or crystal permeated by sunlight. It describes the contemplative process of withdrawing from the senses to meet God in the "divine abyss" of the spirit.</summary>
<keywords>inner beauty, new birth, divine abyss, mysticism, spiritual light, uncreated state, soul’s essence</keywords>

# THE THIRD PART.

...created ground, and draws it into the uncreated state <term>ongeschapenheid: the mystical concept that the essence of the human soul existed in the mind of God before time began and can return to this eternal state</term>, so that the pure spirit of man thus becomes one with Him; if it were possible that a person could see themselves, they would see themselves so exceedingly noble in God that they would completely believe they were God Himself, and would see themselves a hundred thousand times nobler than they are in their own nature.

Another may spend his precious time,
To clothe the heavy body with finery,
So that the beast, in an artificial appearance,
Might be the fairest and most esteemed child.
While the Soul, which remains eternally in life,
Is miserably surrounded by darkness,
And instead of the image of the Divinity, after death,
Must bear the serpent’s image <term>slangen beeld: a reference to the fallen, sinful nature of man, often associated with the serpent in Eden</term>, all too wretchedly.
We wish only to adorn the inwardness,
And not celebrate flesh and blood—that beast—so highly;
That donkey <note>original: "dien ezel" — a common mystical metaphor for the physical body, which carries the soul but is considered lowly, stubborn, and mortal.</note> may go its unworthy way;
One may freely mock and despise it,
We do not wish to attend to its fallen state:
If only the new image may grow upon the Soul,
And be prepared as a temple,
In which God dwells with His Majesty.

JESUS AND THE SOUL.
Page 142

### *The Soul contemplates the Royal government of God.*

The high heaven is Your throne,
And the earth a footstool for Your feet;
Yes King, whom all Angels greet,
To You belong the Scepter and the Crown;
Just as in the Realm of the Seraphim <term>Seraphim: the highest order of angels in celestial hierarchy, known for their burning love for God</term>,
So beautifully also in the human heart,
When it becomes Your heaven,
Where You come to appear as King;
Then the flesh becomes Your footstool <note>original: "voetſchabel" — an archaic term for a low stool or footrest, emphasizing the body's submission to the spirit.</note>,
And submissive to the powers,
Which wait upon the King's signals;
And fly at His high command.
Then the outward limbs become,
A slave to the Royal mind,
Then the serpent in flesh and blood,
Has its head crushed all day long <note>A reference to the biblical promise in Genesis 3:15 that the "seed of the woman" would crush the head of the serpent, interpreted here as the soul overcoming its sinful nature.</note>,
Until the sharp sting of death,
Destroys its den of plunder and grinds it to ash,
Then the Soul has outgrown the danger,
And is eternally beyond all need.

## Divine Answer.

The Kingdom of God does not come with outward appearance, nor shall they say, look here, or look there: for behold, the Kingdom of God is within you.
Luke XVII: verses 20, 21.

->JESUS AND THE SOUL.<-

## On the XXXIII. Emblem.

### *Of the Kingdom of God.*

It is written: *Heaven is my throne, and the Earth is my footstool.* <note>Referencing Isaiah 66:1 or Acts 7:49.</note> When the human mind is God’s heaven and throne, then God holds that flesh in subjection, and thus it is His footstool.

Christ says: *But seek first the Kingdom of God, and His righteousness, and all these things shall be added unto you. Matthew VI: 33.*

That is to say, seek only the true God, who alone is that true eternal kingdom which is, in truth, given to every servant of God. And for this kingdom every person prays daily in his <term>Our Father</term> <note>original: "Vader ons"; referring to the Lord's Prayer.</note>, which prayer is so excellent, noble, good, and useful, that you yourself do not even know what you are asking. God is His own kingdom, and in that kingdom He reigns in all intelligent creatures; thus, that for which we pray is, in truth, God Himself with all His riches. In that same kingdom God becomes our Father, and here His fatherly faithfulness and His fatherly power are proven, provided that He truly finds a place within us to work His noble work; therein the name of God is truly hallowed, magnified, and made known. This is His being hallowed within us: that He might truly govern and rule us, and perform His noble work without any hindrance; then there occurs...

<summary>This commentary explains that the Kingdom of God is not a physical place but a spiritual state where God reigns over the human mind and body. It interprets the Lord's Prayer as a request for God to manifest His presence and work within the soul.</summary>
<keywords>Kingdom of God, Matthew 6:33, Lord's Prayer, spiritual indwelling, hallowing of God's name, divine sovereignty, Jan Luyken</keywords>

--- Page 169 ---
<meta>This page continues the theological reflection on the Lord's Prayer from page 144, specifically focusing on the phrase "Thy will be done" and the nature of the Kingdom of God as an internal state rather than a physical location.</meta>

# THE THIRD PART.

his will on earth as in heaven, that is, in us, as in himself, in the heaven which he himself is.

The kingdom of God is naked, pure God alone, and nothing else. For when all attachment <note>original: "aankleevendheid"; refers to the spiritual concept of being "attached" or "clung" to worldly desires or the ego.</note> is cast off altogether by pure people, then the will of God truly occurs on earth as in Heaven, just as the Father has willed it from eternity in heaven—that is, in his Son. This means that when a person stands in such a way that he intends, desires, and wills nothing else but the will of God, he himself becomes God’s Kingdom, and God truly reigns in him. There the eternal Lord sits upon his Royal Throne, and commands and reigns over the person entirely according to all his will. This noble Kingdom is properly in the innermost ground <term>ground of the soul</term> <note>original: "allerbinnenfte grond"; a mystical term referring to the deepest, most essential part of a person where they can meet God.</note> of the human being.

If we now wish to seek and find the Kingdom of God, we must lose ourselves and all foreign cares, for Christ the Son of God has spoken:
> *Whoever loses his Soul, shall save it.* Mark VIII: 35.

This occurs in a denial of oneself; that is, that a person goes beyond himself, sinking down to the bottom of all that in which he finds himself, both within and without. May God grant us this.

<summary>This page defines the Kingdom of God as a state of pure union with God where human will is entirely replaced by Divine will. It concludes that to find this internal kingdom, one must undergo self-denial and "lose the soul" to find it in God.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the abstract definition of God’s Kingdom to the practical application of living within that Kingdom. While the previous page focused on the internal "ground of the soul," this page illustrates how a soul transformed by God responds to external suffering and enemies through the virtue of serenity.</meta>

JESUS AND THE SOUL.
<meta>catchword: We</meta>

->*The Soul receives everything in serenity from the hand of God.*<-

In whom such a sting still sticks,
Through the evil powers of the old Adam, <note>The "old Adam" is a traditional term for the fallen human nature that remains prone to sin and ego.</note>
That he takes revenge upon his enemy,
With deeds, words, or thoughts;
He acts like a vicious dog,
Who, deprived of right understanding,
Attacks with his mouth the stone,
That was thrown at his body:
A Divine image <note>Referring to the human being restored to the likeness of God.</note> does no such beastliness,
Whatever approaches him of cross or suffering;
Whatever malice imparts to him,
Though the sorrow strikes from all sides,
He looks to God, in sweet and sour,
He receives it all from God’s hands,
And takes no revenge on any creature;
He is honored with damage and disgrace,
And thanks his Creator night and day,
That he, in a life of suffering,
May resemble his God a little.
Ah Savior! please forgive them,
Though it is their evil, it is my good,
Forgive them these unwise deeds;
Thus a Christian falls at God’s feet,
And pleads out of love for those who do evil.

### Divine Answer.

I tell you that you should not resist the evil one: but whoever strikes you on the right cheek, turn the other to him also. Matthew 5:39.

<note>original: "Jezus, Ziel, gelaatenheid, Adams booze krachten, Goddelyk Antwoord, Heiland, Christen, Matth. V: 39"</note>

->JESUS AND THE SOUL.<-

### On the 34th Emblem.

#### On defenseless equanimity, and how one must look to God alone in all occurrences.
<term>equanimity</term> (the original Dutch *gelaatenheid* refers to a state of calm, patient resignation and surrender to God’s will)

If a bear or a dog is pricked with a spear or hit with a thrown stone, it bites at it with great fury, as if the spear or the stone had done it. But a Christian shall not do so; rather, they shall receive everything from God equally with patience, and not avenge themselves with anger and fury upon their opponent, nor curse those who have insulted or despised them, but bless those who curse them. For just as the spear and the stone cannot do anything without the one who wields them, so also wicked people—who are used by God as spears and stones—may do nothing without God. This is not to say that God forces the wicked to commit evil and harm the pious, but He permits it for the trial of the pious.
<note>trial: The original word "oefening" implies both spiritual exercise and a test of character.</note>

God does not force a single person, but since without God no sparrow falls to the earth, and no hair from our head, we shall take all things equally from God: the good and the bad, the sweet and the sour. We shall take it from God and not from the spear or the stone—that is, from wicked people—for they can do nothing without God's permission.
<note>sparrow: the text uses the word "Mosken," an older Dutch term for a sparrow, referencing Matthew 10:29.</note>

Now here is a necessary point to note, in which many people err: wicked people cannot harm me without God, or without God's will. Thus, if someone were to harm my body severely, or to strangle me, God would have foreseen that from eternity; yet through such foreknowledge, the murderer was not forced, as if he had to do it; for it was not necessary, because he could indeed choose not to do it. God forces no one into wickedness; the own...

<meta>catchword: wicked</meta>

<summary>This page explains the theological concept of divine permission, using the metaphor of a spear and a stone to argue that Christians should look past their human persecutors to see God's overarching hand in their trials. It emphasizes that while God foresees all events and permits suffering for spiritual growth, He does not force individuals to commit evil acts.</summary>
<keywords>patience, divine permission, equanimity, providence, metaphors of suffering, theodicy, Christian conduct</keywords>

--- Page 173 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 148 regarding how a Christian should respond to harm caused by others, explaining the distinction between God's permission of evil and His active will.</meta>

# THE THIRD PART.

...an evil will does it without the will of God; thus, evil happens against the will of God, and yet nothing can happen without God. God permits and allows it <note>The Dutch "gehengt" implies a passive permission or "giving rein" to something.</note> so that evil occurs, but He forces no one to do it.

When an evil person harms you, God knows of it, and it does not happen without Him. God knows all things and sees all things, and also permits all things, for He forces no one; therefore, a great and terrible punishment shall follow for the wicked. But if God, through His **providence**, had forced them to evil, then the wicked would be punished unfairly; for then God would be at fault and not the human being, who would have been forced to work evil.

These wicked people are nothing other than spears, sticks, and stones with which God allows the **pious** <note>original: "vroomen" – referring to the devout or God-fearing believers</note> to be struck. Therefore, He shall also justly cast them into that eternal fire; there they have punishment enough. For this reason, the pious need not take revenge on their adversary; indeed, they shall have compassion for their enemies and pray for them: *Oh Father, forgive them, for they know not what they do; do not hold these sins against them.* We shall have more pity for our enemies than for ourselves—we who suffer harm—for we have a reward in heaven, but they have terrifying pain in hell. Therefore, we shall pray for them that they might be converted; it is always better to suffer injustice than to commit injustice. Christ says: *Do not resist evil, but overcome that evil with good; if someone strikes you on the right cheek, offer him the left also. If someone takes your coat, let him have your cloak as well; and if someone forces you to go one mile with him, go two miles with him.* For whoever wishes to serve God and be **surrendered** <term>surrendered: translated from "gelaaten," a central concept in Dutch and German mysticism (Gelassenheit) referring to a state of total yielding to God's will</term>, must be surrendered and subject to both evil and good creatures.

<sig>K 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>This page explains that while God permits evil acts to test the pious, He does not force the wicked to commit them, thus maintaining human responsibility and divine justice. It concludes with an exhortation to non-resistance and practicing "gelaatenheid" (surrender) toward all circumstances.</summary>
<keywords>providence, free will, non-resistance, Sermon on the Mount, equanimity, gelaatenheid, suffering, forgiveness</keywords>

JEZUS AND THE SOUL.

## *The Soul embraces the poor and suffering life of* JESUS CHRIST.

The less comfort in all things,
The more comfort in the only Good,
Which comes pressing through like a spring,
And quenches the thirst of the burning mind.
Away with money, and goods, and earthly treasures,
Away with honor, and status, and ease,
And all that the flesh wishes to grasp,
You are a heavy burden to the Spirit.
O welcome cross, and bitter suffering,
Contempt, dishonor, mockery and scorn,
Rejection from every side,
Forgotten poverty, struggle and hate,
You must eat at my table,
And stay overnight in my house;
Those others <note>The "others" refers to the earthly comforts and honors mentioned in the second stanza.</note> I wish to forget entirely,
And consider them as wicked rabble;
Your good friendship may well endure,
As long as I dwell in the earthly body:
You separate me from the creatures, <note>In this context, "creatures" (Dutch: *kreatuuren*) refers to all created, worldly things as opposed to the Creator.</note>
And ensure that I remain united
With God, my Beloved, my most beautiful,
With whom I desire to dwell eternally.

JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### On the XXXV. Emblem.

### *Concerning the poor and suffering life of Christ.*

All the distress of poverty is easily consumed by the fire of love. It is sweet to the lover to become poor with the Son of God. Every burden is light that love commands one to carry, and to no one will the labor be heavy if he is strengthened with the bread of love. O Lord, you have spoken well; I pray you, let it happen as you have said. You have given good counsel; give also strong help. Let it all together become sweet, what to the flesh seems heavy, and let the burden become light which before seemed unbearable. Let my flesh rejoice in the living God, and my spirit in God its Savior. Oh, how good is God to Israel <note>In this context, "Israel" refers to the spiritual community of the faithful who remain true to God.</note> to those who are upright of heart! O Lord, I will love you, my strength, my fortress, my refuge, and my redeemer. I will also love holy poverty, humility, and patience <note>The original Dutch *lydzaamheid* implies a patient, submissive endurance of suffering or hardship.</note>, following your way, and the way of your holy commandments. I will henceforth hold poverty above all riches, humility above all glory or honor, and patience above all rest. Let me take care for this, and let all things please me that the spirit seeks: for the flesh profits nothing at all. Let me find contentment in this as if in all riches, and let the mouth of the proud life <note>A reference to the arrogant or worldly way of living that mocks spiritual devotion.</note> not disturb me; for they shall fall when their day comes, and their end shall be without honor; but I will rejoice in my Lord, and in you, O God my Savior, I will exult, for you have become for me a mirror of poverty, humility, and patience.

<meta>The catchword "Men" indicates that the following page likely begins with a discussion of how "One" (Men) ought to live or perceive these virtues.</meta>

<summary>This page contains a devotional meditation on embracing poverty, humility, and patient suffering as a means of imitating Christ. The speaker asks for divine strength to find sweetness in hardships that the "flesh" finds difficult, viewing Christ as the ultimate "mirror" of these virtues.</summary>
<keywords>poverty, imitation of Christ, humility, patience, spiritual love, flesh vs spirit, devotional prayer, Jan Luyken</keywords>

--- Page 177 ---
# THE THIRD PART.

Men say: It used to be a comfort to the wretched when they had a companion in suffering. Who is that companion, so good and godly, who knows how to have compassion for the wretched? That is Jesus Christ our Lord, who suffered for us and was crucified.

Truly, it is a great comfort for those who are sorrowful and tormented in various ways, that Christ was also tempted, saddened, and tormented with much pain for us.

To many people it seems a hard word when it is said: Deny yourself, take up your cross, and follow JESUS <note>A reference to the Gospel of Matthew 16:24, a core principle of "The Imitation of Christ."</note>. But it will be much harder to hear at the Last Judgment: 
> Depart from me, you cursed ones, into the eternal fire. <note>original: "Gaat van my gy vervloekten in 't eeuwige vuur," citing Matthew 25:41.</note>
For those who now gladly hear of bearing the cross, and follow that path, they will not then fear that horrific word of eternal damnation.

The sign of the cross will be in the sky when our Lord comes for judgment. And then all the servants of the cross, who have crucified themselves here with Christ <note>This refers to the practice of "mortification," or suppressing one's own ego and worldly desires.</note>, will come before the same Judge, Christ, with great confidence.

Why then do you now shrink from bearing the cross, through which one enters the eternal Kingdom? In the cross salvation is found; in the cross is life; in the cross lies protection against enemies; in the cross lies the infusion of heavenly sweetness; in the cross is the strength of the heart; in the cross lies the joy of the Spirit.

<keywords>Cross, Jesus Christ, comfort, companion, damnation, salvation, Kingdom of God, joy of the Spirit</keywords>

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### On the XXXV. Emblem.

#### *On the Poor and Suffering Life of Christ.*

All the distress of poverty is easily consumed by the fire of love. It is sweet for the lover <note>The "lover" refers to the soul who is in a state of mystical love for God.</note> to become poor with the Son of God. Every burden is light that love commands us to carry, and to no one will the labor be heavy, if they are strengthened by the bread of love. O Lord, You have spoken well; I pray You, let it be as You have said. You have given good counsel; give also strong help. Let all that seems heavy to the flesh become sweet, and let the burden become light that before seemed unbearable. Let my flesh rejoice in the living God, and my spirit in God its Savior. Oh, how good is God to Israel <note>In this context, "Israel" refers to the spiritual community of the faithful, those who are "pure of heart."</note>, to those who are upright of heart! O Lord, I will love You, my strength, my fortress, my refuge, and my redeemer. I will also love holy poverty, humility, and patience <term>patience: originally "lydzaamheid," which means a quiet, submissive endurance of suffering or hardship</term>, following Your way and the way of Your holy commandments. Henceforth I will hold poverty above all riches, humility above all glory or honor, and patience above all rest. Let me care for this, and let all things please me that the spirit seeks; for the flesh counts for nothing <note>A reference to John 6:63: "It is the spirit that gives life; the flesh profits nothing."</note>. Let me find contentment in this as in all riches, and let the mouth of the proud life not touch me; for they shall fall when their day comes, and their end shall be without honor; but I will rejoice in my Lord, and in You, O God my Savior, I will be glad, for You have become for me a mirror of poverty, humility, and patience.

They say: It used to be a comfort to the wretched when they have a companion in suffering. Who is this companion, so good and godly, who knows how to have compassion for the wretched? It is Jesus Christ our Lord, who suffered for us and was crucified.

It is truly a great comfort for those who are sorrowful and tormented in various ways, that Christ was also tempted, sorrowful, and tormented with much pain for us.

It seems a hard word to many people when it is said:
> Deny yourself, take up your cross, and follow JESUS.
But it will be much harder to hear at the Last Judgment <term>Last Judgment: the "jongste gericht," referring to the final assessment of humanity by God at the end of time</term>:

### *The Soul rests on Jesus' breast.*

Even if all things conspired together,
That they would disturb my rest;
It was in vain, as you can see,
They did not rob me of my rest.
The lesser must fall silent for the greater,
The smallest must bow before the greatest.
I rest so safely and so sweetly,
In *God*, my beloved, the highest good.
And He is greater than all of them;
How then could my rest ever fail me?
Even if the whole creation stands
In the pangs of birth, in flame and fire, <note>The phrase "baarens nood" (birth-throes) refers to the biblical imagery of the world groaning in travail, as found in Romans 8:22.</note>
And brings forth much misery and much pain,
We lie quietly on *Jesus'* heart.
That is a place where every evil,
However great and strong, passes by;
A place of true peace and joys,
The reward of unadulterated virtues.
O Deepest *Love* from *God’s* heart!
My *Jesus*, oh how sweet you are!

### *Divine Answer.*

> Peace I leave with you, my peace I give unto you: not as the world gives, give I unto you. Let not your heart be troubled, neither let it be afraid. *John 14:27.*

### On the XXXVI. Emblem.

#### *Of True Rest.*

John lay upon the breast of our most sweet Lord Jesus; <note>A reference to John 13:23, where the "beloved disciple" reclines next to Jesus at the Last Supper.</note> when a loving soul reads this, her heart is set on fire.

Oh! How sweet it is to rest upon the heart of Jesus! Jesus is the eternal love of the Father, and the eternal, essential gentleness; wrath and severity are swallowed up by him, and all turmoil must subside before him.

Whoever desires to rest in this holy place must abandon all false rest founded upon created things <term>schepfelen|the Dutch word refers to all things in creation (people, objects, status) as opposed to the Creator</term>.

You are greatly led astray if you seek rest anywhere but in God.

He who rests in God has nothing to fear, for God is greater than all created things. But he who rests in any created thing stands in constant fear that his peace will be disturbed and broken by another created thing; and where there is still fear, there is no true rest.

> Psalm XCI. *He who dwells in the secret place of the Most High shall abide under the shadow of the Almighty.* <note>original: "Die in de schuilplaats des Allerhoogsten is gezeten, die zal vernachten in de schaduwe des Almachtigen."</note>
>
> *I will say to the Lord: My refuge and my fortress; my God, in whom I trust.*
>
> *For he shall deliver you from the snare of the fowler, and from the deadly pestilence.*

# THE THIRD PART.

*He shall cover you with his feathers, and under his wings shall you trust: his truth shall be a shield and buckler.* <note>original: "rondasse en beukelaar." These are two types of shields; a "rondasse" is a circular shield, while a "beukelaar" (buckler) is a small, round shield held in the hand to deflect blows.</note>

*For the Pestilence that walks in darkness: for the destruction that wastes at noonday.*

*Only with your eyes shall you behold it: and you shall see the reward of the wicked.*

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

->*The Soul has sealed her heart with*<-
->*Jesus.*<-

While others seal with their signet rings,
A great hoard of worldly things,
Which they hold dear and brings them pleasure,
To guard against a servant’s theft of treasure;
I have the Son from God the Father,
That living fountain and the vein,
Of deepest love and tender mercy,
The eternal light of Majesty,
The sweetest JESUS, Lord of Lords,
Whom all the blessed spirits honor
And serve, without compulsion or law,
So clearly set upon my heart
As if a Seal; so that those gifts,
Which we so freely received from God,
Might never be snatched away from us,
By him who lurks by that treasure chest,
Intending to make himself the master.
The robber <note>A common reference to the Devil or the "thief of souls."</note> dares not come near us,
When he sees that exalted seal,
For this roast <note>original: "gebraad." A metaphor for the soul; the "heat" of the divine seal makes the soul "distasteful" or untouchable to the predator.</note> is not to his taste.

## Divine Answer.

Set me as a seal upon your heart, as a seal upon your arm: for love is strong as death; ardor <note>original: "iver" (zeal/jealousy). In the context of this verse, it refers to the fierce, possessive intensity of divine love.</note> is as hard as the grave: its coals are fiery coals, flames of the Lord. Song of Solomon 8:6.
