# THE THIRD PART.

...ly lover <note>The text begins mid-word, likely continuing "goddelijke" (godly) or "geestelijke" (spiritual) from the previous page</note>, and therefore I bear his marks <note>original: "lidteekenen", referring to the stigmata or the "marks of Jesus" mentioned in Galatians 6:17</note> burned or engraved upon my body, so that I may know him manifoldly, internally and externally; that I, with heart and work, may be present not as my own, but as Christ’s, and that he may live in me as the crucified one; for I am fastened with Christ to the cross, and I always carry that dying of the Lord JESUS about in my body, so that the life of the Lord JESUS might also be made manifest in my body. Just as it is said that the servants of the Antichrist shall have that mark of the beast on their right hand or on their forehead: so shall those souls united with Christ bear that mark of the living God not only on their foreheads, or on their hands, or other members of the body, but inwardly in the heart; just as we have borne the image of the earthly, so shall we also bear the image of the heavenly.

Our heart must be like a <term>coin</term> <note>original: "penning". In this period, coins were often used as metaphors for the soul's value being derived solely from the "image" of the Sovereign (God) stamped upon it</note> of the highest King, which is marked with his image. Our Savior desires that we shall have him as a seal in our contemplation and works, and that his mark be impressed upon our words as well as our works; for thus they shall be Royal and not false coins, being those which are marked with the King’s image: just as coins now have their value from the King’s image, so do the works and thoughts which come from the heart have their merit only from Christ; and as much as a heart is conformed to its Lord, whose image it bears, so much will it be acceptable to God.

<summary>This page describes the mystical process of "sealing" the heart with the image of Christ, comparing the believer’s internal transformation to the minting of a royal coin. It emphasizes that a soul's value and the merit of its works depend entirely on how closely they reflect the "image" of the Savior.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the previous discussion on the soul bearing the "seal" or "image" of Christ like a coin. The "Rock" represents the wounds of Christ, a common theme in 17th-century Pietist and contemplative literature.</meta>

JESUS AND THE SOUL.

## *The Soul places her trust in the wounds of Christ.*

Who is that Rock of Stone, in whose crevices the little Dove
hides,
When the Hawk, hoarse with murder and bloodlust,
howls?
And who is the little Dove? ah, my Jesus, my beloved!
Were you not the Rock, I would never have found it,
And is my poor Soul not the defenseless little Dove,
That, shy of eternal death, flees into your
wounds?
Oh yes, my only salvation, for outside of your wounds,
I would soon be devoured by the Eagle of the abyss <note>A metaphorical reference to the Devil or the threat of damnation</note>,
Where would I go? alas! he would seize me in one
flight,
And scatter, all too cruelly, my little feathers in the air.
All my righteousness would not avail me,
My strengths would fail and desert me,
Therefore my God, my Love, you are the precious stone
Of my salvation, where I hide; it is you and you alone,
You are the All, and I the Nothing, my most beautiful Lord,
Let me daily learn your strength and my weakness.
My God, my most beautiful Love, my Jesus, my all,
Your tokens of love, red with the holy heavenly blood,
Your tokens of love in your feet and your hands <note>Referring to the stigmata or the wounds sustained during the Crucifixion</note>,
Make the fire of love in return burn so within my Soul,
That I heartily wish for the outward death,
So that my spirit might pour itself entirely into your Spirit,
Like the water of a River, which, having sprung from the Sea,
Does not rest until it has flowed back into its origin.

---

JEZUS AND THE SOUL.
<meta>catchword: innermost</meta>

## On the XXXVIII. Emblem.

### ->*Of Christ's wounds.*<-

**C**ome, let us, along with the loving Soul, in the temptations, misery, and sorrow of this life, take refuge in the hollows of the Rock: what Rock? JESUS CHRIST our Lord: For this is the Rock that was struck by Moses <note>A reference to the biblical account in Exodus 17 and Numbers 20, where Moses strikes a rock to provide water for the Israelites</note>—that is, by the Jewish people—with the rod of the Cross, and gave forth abundant water; so much so that one was able to suck not only waters, but according to the testimony of Holy Scripture, also Oil from this hardest stone <note>A reference to Deuteronomy 32:13: "and he made him to suck honey out of the rock, and oil out of the flinty rock"</note>. Therefore the Prophet Jeremiah also said; *Leave the cities—that is, the noise and the unrest of the people—and dwell in the Rock, inhabitants of Moab: and become like a Dove, that nests in the mouth of a high cave.* <note>Quoting Jeremiah 48:28</note> That is, in the openings <note>original: "opende," referring to the wounds</note> of Christ. This is the Stone which the Patriarch Jacob set up as a sign, and upon which he poured Oil, as a proof of overflowing grace and devotion. What can we lack in this Rock, for here we are safe and free from all our enemies: Here the old serpent, that cunning and twisting snake, cannot approach: Here we are taken up from the earth, and set upon the way to heaven; though the world attacks us, though the enemy threatens us, though the flesh complains, we truly have nothing to fear, since we are founded upon the Rock: nowhere can we be so safe and free as in the wounds of our Lord.

What I lack in myself, I boldly draw from the innermost part of my Lord, for his innermost...

<vocab>Emblem, Christ's wounds, Rock, Jesus Christ, Moses, Jeremiah, Moab, Jacob, Devotion</vocab>

<summary>This page provides a theological commentary on Emblem 38, interpreting Christ as the biblical "Rock of Ages." It links various Old Testament figures like Moses, Jacob, and Jeremiah to the concept of finding spiritual safety within the wounds of Jesus.</summary>

HET DERDE DEEL. <note>original: "THE THIRD PART"</note>

L 3

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

innermost part is full and overflowing with grace, and it does not lack openings for flowing out; his hands and feet have been pierced through, and his side struck through with a lance, so that through these crevices I can suck honey from the Rock, and oil from a hard stone <note>A repetition of the biblical imagery from Deuteronomy 32:13 used on the previous page, symbolizing finding sweetness and sustenance in the midst of suffering.</note>; that is, I can through this taste and see how kind the Lord is. He thought thoughts of peace toward me, and I did not know it: but the nail that penetrated his body has become a key for me, to see the will of the Lord. How should I not see it? The nail cries out, the wound cries out, that God is truly reconciling himself with the world in Christ. The iron has pierced through his soul, and has come even to his heart, in such a way that he now cannot do otherwise than have compassion for our weaknesses. 

The secret of his heart has become manifest through the holes of his body; that great mystery of godliness <term>godliness (Godzaligheid): the state of being devout and pious; here referring to the "mystery of faith" revealed through Christ</term> has been uncovered; the depths of the mercy of our God, with which the dayspring from on high has visited us <note>A reference to the Song of Zechariah in Luke 1:78, describing Christ as the rising sun bringing light to those in darkness.</note>, have come to light, and how should they not come forth through the wounds? For in what could it shine more clearly than in your wounds, that you, Lord, are kind and gracious, and of great mercy?

Longinus <note>According to tradition, Longinus was the name of the Roman soldier who pierced Jesus' side with a spear (the Holy Lance) while he hung on the cross.</note> has opened Christ's side for me with the lance, and I have entered into it. Here I live safely, here I delight myself, here I rest sweetly, here I graze with joy.

->Longinus, Christ, Godliness, Mercy, Lance, Rock<-

# JESUS AND THE SOUL.

### *The Soul in longing to be dissolved, and to be with Christ.* <note>The title alludes to Philippians 1:23: "I desire to depart [be dissolved] and be with Christ, which is better by far."</note>

Who asks what moves me to cheerfully
->sing,<-
While much misery and much sickness press upon me?
O blind reason! that cross, that adversity and strife,
Is itself the wellspring of my joy and sweetness.
Nothing is there that keeps me from God, my Love, separated,
But an old wall <note>A metaphor for the physical body or the mortal self that separates the soul from the divine.</note>, which still stands between us.
My flesh, the "old man," <term>old man (ouden mensch): a biblical concept from St. Paul referring to the sinful, worldly nature of a person before spiritual rebirth</term> as I see him perish,
From day to day, let storm and tempest strike him;
That awakens a joy in me, until he is utterly
Ruined by blow and buffet, and no longer ->found,<-
Then I fall into God—the fountain for which I thirst so—
Into His arms, safely at home, and rest upon His breast;
There beats that loving heart, that became human on
->earth,<-
And reborn the fallen Soul as an heir.
Then my spirit melts like wax in JESUS’ fire of love,
We become entirely united. What He is by nature,
And from all eternity, that I become by grace;
Thus can the hunger of the soul truly satisfy itself.
O blind world, awake! behold what you do,
How you lose the highest good for smoke and wind. <note>“Smoke and wind” is a common seventeenth-century Dutch idiom for the vanity and transience of worldly possessions.</note>

---

## Divine Answer.

To him who overcomes, I will give the right to sit with me on my throne, just as I overcame and sat down with my Father on his throne. Revelation 3:21.

THE THIRD PART.

# S O N G.

->TUNE: Amarilli, my beautiful.<- <note>original: "Amarille mia Belle." A famous Italian madrigal by Giulio Caccini (1602), frequently used as a melody for spiritual contrafacta—sacred lyrics set to popular secular tunes.</note>

**JESUS CHRIST** my treasure!
You are everything, and all that I desire.
My Bridegroom, my Lord,
You are everything, in heaven and on earth;
Whom your wisdom brought forth.
How long will it last before I shall dwell with you?
Jesus Christ, Jesus Christ, Jesus Christ, my
beautiful one!

Like a deer that has fled,
Into the thick woods, from the pursuit of its tormentors,
From dogs and from hunters,
And out of hot thirst, cries out for the water-streams,
Which come from the mountains,
So my Soul cries out to you, O God exalted! <note>This stanza is a poetic paraphrase of Psalm 42:1: "As the deer pants for streams of water, so my soul pants for you, my God."</note>
Jesus Christ, Jesus Christ, Jesus Christ, my life.

O perishing and dying,
Could you entirely devour my own self-will!
Then there would be found in the heart:
A living water, sprung from God's heart;
No thirst would pain me anymore.
Yes, sweetest Jesus, my most beautiful Lord,
Let me die, let me die, let me die, [and] learn
from you. <note>In mystical tradition, "dying" often refers to "mortification"—the process of killing off one's selfish desires so that the divine can live within the person.</note>

### On the XXXIX. Emblem. <note>original: "Zinnebeeld" — An emblem is a symbolic illustration accompanied by a poem or prose that explains a moral or religious lesson, a very popular genre in the 17th century.</note>

Now, when the God-loving Soul has come to the end of the way of her pilgrimage, through the dangerous wild wilderness of this world, and the old partition wall of her outer body has fallen away from before her face, then she goes back into her first and right fatherland, from where she sprouted (though in Adam she had wandered out), home to her most beloved Father, to play before his face for all eternity with all the holy angels.

To say something then about the blessed Fatherland, its essence, joy, and glory, we shall yet at the end weave a little wreath <note>original: "Roozenhoetje" — Literally a "little hat of roses," but used here to mean a collection or garland of beautiful spiritual thoughts.</note> of various high-colored little flowers, gathered from the soul-refreshing Rose Garden of a highly enlightened friend of God, <note>This likely refers to the German mystic Jacob Böhme, whose works frequently used the imagery of the "Rose Garden" and "Divine contemplation."</note> who speaks thus from high and deep Divine contemplation:

Would we then be blind to God? The Spirit of Christ sees through and in us what he wills: and what he wills, that we see and know in him; outside of him we know nothing of God. He does Divine works, and he sees when and what he wills: not when Adam <note>In this context, "Adam" refers to the "old man" or fallen human nature that seeks its own will and pride.</note> wills, when Adam would gladly shake off his wickedness to let himself be seen with pride.

Oh no: he hides himself there; he does not see in us, in the light of joy, in God: but in the cross, in tribulation, in the suffering and dying of Christ, in

He looks into Christ's persecution and contempt, in great sadness: therein he sees: and he lets the old Donkey <note>original: "ouden Ezel" — A common mystical metaphor for the physical body or the lower, carnal nature of man, which must labor and carry the "cross" of earthly existence.</note> tremble and carry the cross of Christ, for that is its office. But, on the way through the death of Christ, the new Man looks even into the world of Angels; that is easier and clearer for him to understand than the earthly world; it happens naturally.

Not through imagination: but with seeing eyes—with those eyes that shall possess the world of Angels; namely, with the eyes of the Soul's image.

With the Spirit that proceeds from the fire of the Souls, that same Spirit looks into Heaven; it sees God and eternity, and none other; it is also that noble image after God’s likeness.

But when we wish to speak of Paradise, and its joy, and highest essence, what that may be, we have no likeness in this world; we would need the tongues of Angels for that, and the knowledge of Angels; and even if we had them, we could not speak of it with this [earthly] tongue. In the mind—as the Soul travels upon the Paradisiacal Bridal Chariot <note>The "Bridal Chariot" symbolizes the soul’s ecstatic union with the Divine, often described in mystical literature as a marriage between the soul and Christ.</note>—it is well understood; but with the tongue we cannot lift it up. Nevertheless, we do not wish to throw away the A B C, but meanwhile speak with the Children, until another mouth for speaking shall be given to us.

The Creation, or the whole of creaturely existence, is nothing else than a revelation of the all-present, unfathomable God; everything that He is in His eternal, beginningless Birth and governance, that same thing is also the creation—though not in [the same] omnipotence and power, but just as an Apple grows on a tree: it is not the tree itself, but it grows out of the power of the tree. For God did not bring forth **creatureliness** <note>original: "Kreatuurlykheid" — the state of being a created being or the physical world itself.</note> so that He might thereby become perfect, but for His own revelation, as a great joy and glory: not that such joy first began with the **Creation**: no, for it has been from eternity in the great **Mystery** <note>original: "Myſterio," likely referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*, a term used by Jacob Böhme to describe the chaotic, unmanifested "nothing" from which all things are born.</note>, but only as a spiritual playing within itself. **Creatureliness** or Creation is that same play [emerging] out of itself, as a Model or Tool of the eternal spirit, with which He plays; and it is just like a great **Harmony** of many kinds of stringed instruments, which is arranged as into a single Harmony. For the eternal word or divine sound or voice, which is a Spirit, has introduced itself into formations as into an **outspoken word** <note>A Böhmean concept where the "Speaking Word" of God manifests as the physical, "outspoken" world.</note> or sound along with the **generation** <note>original: "gebaaring" — the process of divine birthing or manifestation.</note> of the great Mystery; and just as the joy-play is within the Spirit of the eternal generation in itself, so also is the instrument—as that outspoken Form within itself—which carries the living sound and strikes with its own eternal spirit of the will, so that it rings and sounds. Just as an **Organ** of many voices is worked by a single wind, so that every voice, indeed every pipe, gives its tone, and yet there is but one kind of air which gives sound in every voice, according to how the Instrument or Organ is made. So it is in eternity: in the entire work of the divine revelation there is but one single Spirit, which is the **Revealer** <note>original: "Openbaarder"</note> in the outspoken sound, as well as in the speaking sound of God, which is the life of the great Mystery and of all that which is born from it; He is the Revealer of all the works of God.

The voice of God carries its joy through the creature, as through an **Instrument**, continually and eternally. So also is the eternal generation of the **Holy Mystery** in great power and restoration, where one divine fruit of great love and desire stands alongside another in the divine essence; and it is like a constant wrestling and blooming of beautiful colors, and a lovely scent from the divine power, according to the property of the divine nature, a constant good taste of love out of divine desire. ### and its Joy.

...the **Son of God**, [they] shall praise, sing, and play, jubilate, and increase the heavenly joy. And where else would the Father ordain them, but before the door of His heart? All the joy of man, which is within the whole of man, springs from the wellspring of the heart; likewise, in God, the great joy springs from the wellspring of His heart. Therefore, He has created the **Holy Angels** out of Himself, who are like little Gods according to the **essence** <term>original: "wezen" — refers to the fundamental substantial reality of a being.</term> and the **qualities** <note>original: "qualiteiten" — in this mystical context, these refer to the seven foundational properties of divine nature that Böhme describes.</note> of the whole of God, so that they shall play in the Divine power, praise, sing, strike upon instruments, and increase the rising joy out of the heart of God.

The true love in the Divine nature springs from the wellspring of the **Son of God**; see, child of man, let it be told to you, the angels already know well what the true love for God is—you have need of it in your cold heart. Note: when the lovely, friendly radiance and the light with the sweet power from the Son of God shines through all powers in the whole Father, then all powers are ignited with the lovely light and the sweet power, triumphing and rich with joy. So too, when that lovely and joyful light of the Son of God shines upon the dear angels and glitters within their hearts, then all powers in their bodies catch fire, and such a joyful fire of love rises up that they praise, sing, and play with great joy, such as I—nor any creature—can express.

The person or the body of a **King of the Angels** is born out of all qualities and out of all powers of his entire kingdom, through the **exhaling Spirit of God** <note>original: "walmende Geest Gods" — "walmende" implies a steaming, breathing, or emanating movement, suggesting the Spirit's active presence flowing through creation.</note>, and therefore he is a King, because his power extends itself into all angels of his entire kingdom.

### Of the Eternal Fatherland,

...and the manifold power of God the Father, and the **King of the Angels** signifies the **Son of the Father**, or the heart out of the Father's powers; and He is also the heart out of all powers from which the Angels are made. The proceeding out from a King of the Angels into his Angels, or the **infusion** <term>original: "inficiring" — refers to the way divine power permeates or influences a creature, filling it with its own nature.</term> of his Angels, signifies **God the Holy Spirit**. Just as He proceeds from the Father and the Son and permeates all the powers of the Father, as well as all Heavenly fruits and forms—from which everything has its rising up, and wherein the Heavenly joy consists—so it is also with the working or power of a **Cherub** or **Throne Angel**. He works in all his Angels just as a Son and Holy Spirit work in all the powers of the Father, or as the **Sun** works in all the powers of the **Stars**. 

From this, all Angels receive the will of the Throne Angel and are all obedient to him, for they all work in his power, and he penetrates into them all with his power. For they are the members of his body, just as all powers of the Father are the Son's members, and He is their heart; and just as all Heavenly forms and fruits are joints <note>original: "gelederen" — implies structural parts or ranks, like joints in a body or ranks in an army.</note> of the Holy Spirit, and He is their heart in which they rise up. Or, just as the Sun is the heart of all the stars, and all stars are members of the Sun, and work among one another as one Star—yet the Sun is the heart within them. Although they are many, in manifold powers, yet everything works in the Sun’s power, and everything has its life from the Sun’s power—look at whatever you will, whether in flesh, or in metals, or in the plants of the earth.

Here you shall know that the Angels are not all of one **Quality**; they are also not all equal to one another in power and might. Every Angel indeed has the power of all **seven Source-Spirits** <term>original: "Welgeesten" — Böhme's term for the seven foundational properties of divine nature that flow like a wellspring to create all reality.</term> within himself, but in each there is somewhat of a specific quality of strength, and according to that same quality, he is also glorified. For just as the **Salitter** <term>original: "Salitter" — a term used by Böhme to describe the divine "saltpetre" or the fundamental spiritual substance of the heavenly kingdom.</term> was in every place at the time of creation, so...

[...in a manifold way] has also become the Angel, and according to the **Quality** <term>original: "Qualiteit" — in this context, it refers to the "source-spirits" or the essential, driving forces of nature (such as heat, light, or bitterness) that constitute a being's character.</term> that is strongest in the Angel, so he is named and glorified.

Just as the flowers of the meadows each receive their color from their Quality, and also have their name according to their Quality, so it is also with the **Holy Angels**: in some, the **astringent** <note>original: "wrange" — can also mean harsh, tart, or sharp; a primary force in this theological system.</note> Quality is the strongest, and those are light brown, and closest to the cold.

When the light of the **Son of God** shines upon them, they are like a brown lightning, entirely clear in their Quality: some are of the Quality of **water**, and those are light, like the Holy Heaven, and when the light shines upon them, it is like a **Crystalline Lake**.

In some, the **bitter** Quality is the strongest; these are like a precious and green stone, which looks like lightning, and when the light shines upon them, it appears out of the red and green as if a **carbuncle** <note>A historic name for a red gemstone, typically a garnet, often described in ancient texts as having its own inner light.</note> were shining out of it, or as if life itself had its origin there.

Some are of the Quality of **heat**, which are the very brightest, yellow and red, and when the light beholds them, it is like the lightning of the Son of God. In some, the Quality of **love** is the strongest; these are a glimpse of **Heavenly joy**, entirely light; when the light shines upon them, it is like a light blue, a lovely sight.

In some, the Quality of **Tone**, or of sound, is the strongest; these are also light, and when the light shines upon them, it is like a rising of lightning, as if something there wished to exalt itself.

Some are of the entire nature, like a common mixture; when the light shines upon them, it is like the **Holy Heaven**, which is formed out of all the spirits of God.

But the **King** is the heart of all Qualities, and has his realm <note>original: "Rivier" — while literally "river," in 17th-century Dutch it can also refer to a territory, reach, or sphere of influence.</note> in the middle like a **wellspring**, just as the Sun stands in the middle among the planets, and is a King—

# Of the Eternal Fatherland

...[Ki]ng of the Stars, and a heart of nature in this world: just as great is the <term>Cherubim</term> <note>original: "Cherubin" — used here as a title for a high-ranking angelic ruler or Throne-Prince.</note> or King of the Angels.

And just as those other six <term>Planets</term> <note>In the cosmology of this era, the Sun was seen as the central heart and ruler among seven planetary bodies (including the Moon and Sun).</note> are commanders alongside the Sun, and give their will to the Sun so that it may rule and work within them, so all the Angels give their will to the King, and the Princes of the Angels are in council with the King.

But you should know here that they possess only a will of love among one another; no one begrudges another’s form and beauty, for just as it happens among the <term>Spirits of God</term> <note>original: "Geesten Gods" — refers to the seven fundamental qualities or creative forces of the divine nature.</note>, so it is also among them. Likewise, they all possess <term>Divine joy</term> at once, and all together enjoy the <term>Heavenly food</term> <note>original: "Hemelse spyze" — spiritual nourishment, often equated with manna or the "Word."</note>; there is no distinction in this.

But in the colors and the strength of their power there is a distinction, yet in their perfection there is none at all, for each one has the power of all the <term>Spirits of God</term> within them; therefore, when the light of the <term>Son of God</term> shines upon them, the quality of each Angel reveals itself through colors.

I have only recounted some of the forms and colors, but there are many more which I shall not write down for the sake of brevity. For just as the Godhead reveals itself in infinity through its uprising <note>original: "opstyge" — likely referring to the dynamic, surging movement of divine life or the "birth" of the qualities within the Godhead.</note>, so there are also unfathomable varieties of colors and forms among the Angels: I can show you no true likeness in this world, save for the blooming earth in May, which is but a dead and earthly example.

What then do the Angels of God do in Heaven, or why, or to what end has God created them?

You greedy ones may take note of this—you who strive in this world after <term>pride</term> <note>original: "hovaardy" — arrogance or haughtiness.</note>, honor, fame, violence, money, and goods, and who wring from the poor their sweat and blood, squandering their labor in ostentation, and thinking yourselves better than the...

upon all kinds of growths, all kinds of figures, all kinds of colors; for the Godhead reveals itself in an infinite and unfathomable variety of colors, forms, and joy.

Now that <term>Spring-Spirit</term> <note>original: "Wel-Geest" — referring to the "wellspring" or source-spirits within the divine nature that provide the vital force for all creation.</note> in the Godhead, which then manifests itself specifically with its rising and wrestling in love—as if it had become the most excellent—that same Prince of the Angels also immediately begins his heavenly <term>music</term> <note>original: "Musica"</note> with his associated angels according to his quality, with singing, playing, piping, and all kinds of heavenly art, which arises within the Spirits of God.

But when the <term>Center</term> <note>original: "Centrum" — in Böhme’s cosmology, the "Center" often refers to the point of divine birth where the light of the Son breaks through the "dark" or "sour" qualities.</note> rises in the midst—that is, when the Birth of the Son of God manifests itself specifically as a triumph—then the music or joy of all three Royal regiments of the entire creation of the Angels arises.

What kind of joy this may be, I leave for every Soul to consider; I cannot grasp it in my corrupted nature, much less write of it. With this song, I summon the reader into the other life; there he himself shall join the <term>procession</term> <note>original: "rei" — a sacred choral dance or rhythmic movement.</note>, and only then believe this Spirit; what he does not understand here, he shall have there in sight.

You shall know that this is not "sucked from a stone" <note>An idiomatic expression meaning this knowledge is not baseless or invented from nothing, but drawn from a real source.</note>, but when a lightning flash arises in the <term>Center</term> <note>original: "Centro"</note>, the Spirit sees and knows it. Therefore, take heed and do not behave too scornfully toward this passage, or you shall be found a mocker before God, and it might go with you as it did with King Lucifer.

What then do the Angels do when they are not singing? Behold, what the Godhead does, they do also. When these powers rise up in God, they move one another and enter into one another in a constant mingling; then, from within them, all kinds of colors emerge, and in those colors all kinds of fruit grow. Just as two things are to be observed in God, so it is in the same manner in the Angel.

First, there is the power, and within the power is the sound; this rises up into the Spirit, into the Head, and into the **Mind** <term>original: "Gemoed." In 17th-century mystical thought, this refers to the inner sanctuary of the soul where will and emotion reside.</term>, just as in Man it rises into the brain. In the Mind, the angel has his open Gate; in the Heart, he has his Seat and his origin, where he springs forth from all the powers. For the **wellspring** <note>original: "Welbron."</note> of all powers wells up in the Heart, just as it does in Man, and in the Head he has his **Regal Seat** <note>original: "Vorstelyken Stoel." A metaphorical throne representing the governing center of consciousness and perception.</note>. From there, he sees everything that is outside of him, and hears everything, and tastes everything, and smells everything, and feels everything.

and the growth of Eternal life, of the magnificence and colors of eternal joy, and of the lovely sight of God: the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit; also of the praiseworthy Brotherhood and fellowship of the Angels, of the everlasting joy, of the Holiness of God, and of their Princely Regiment. Paradise exists in the Divine power, and is nothing physical or tangible <note>original: "begrypelyks," literally "graspable." In this context, it refers to the heavy, opaque matter of the fallen world compared to spiritual substance.</note>, but its physicality is like that of the Angels, where there is a clear, transparent essence, as if it were material—and it *is* material—but shaped purely out of the power, so that everything is transparent and shining, where the **Center of birth** <term>original: "Centrum der geboorte." So is Paradise from eternity to eternity: but because the Light of God is eternal and shines without ceasing or hindrance, there is also in that birth an unchangeable essence, where everything rises up in pure perfection in great love. For that gives the Spirit of knowledge, [showing] that in Paradise there are things growing just as in this World, in such a form, but not in such **distress** <note>original: "kwaal." This refers to the sickness, decay, or "curse" that affects earthly plants and matter.</note> and tangibility.

Just as we see that all kinds of Herbs and Grass grow here from the Earth, receiving their power from the Sun and the Stars: so the Heaven or **Heavenly Limbus** <term>original: "Hemelse Limbus." In theosophical tradition, this refers to the spiritual "soil" or the divine matrix from which the heavenly world is crystallized.</term> is in the place of the Earth, and the light of God is in the place of the Sun, and the eternal Father is in the place of the power of the Stars. The depth or space of this essence is without beginning and end; its width is not to be reached.

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 185 regarding the nature of Paradise, transitioning from its general spiritual "matter" to a specific comparison between the visible physical world and the invisible heavenly realm.</meta>

<page-num>186</page-num>
<header>Of the Eternal Fatherland,</header>

As you look upon this world, you have an example of Heaven: the Stars signify the Angels, for just as the Stars must remain unchanged until the end of this time, so must the Angels remain eternally unchanged in the eternal time of Heaven.

The Elements signify the wonderful proportion and change of the form of Heaven; for just as the space between the Stars and the Earth continually changes in its form—at one moment there is beautiful light, soon it is sorrowful <note>original: "droevig," meaning dark, gloomy, or overcast in this meteorological context</note>, then there is wind, then rain, then snow; one moment the space is blue, then greenish, then white, then dark.

So also is the change of Heaven in manifold colors and forms, but not in such a manner as in this World, but everything according to the rising up of the Spirits of God; and the light of the Son of God shines therein eternally, but there is nevertheless at one time a greater rising in the **birth** <term>original: "geboorte." In this mystical context, it refers to the ongoing, dynamic process of divine manifestation or the unfolding of God’s qualities.</term> than at another; therefore the wonderful wisdom of God is incomprehensible.

The Earth signifies the heavenly nature or the **seventh Nature-spirit** <term>original: "zevende Natuur-geest." In the author's mystical system, this represents the final stage of divine manifestation—the "body" or substantiality of the spiritual world where forms become visible.</term>, in which the images and forms and colors arise: the Birds, Fish, and animals signify the manifold shapes of the Figures in Heaven.

Then you shall know—for the Spirit testifies to it in the flash <note>original: "bliksem." This refers to a sudden moment of divine illumination or intuitive insight</note>—that in Heaven all sorts of figures likewise arise, just like the Animals, Birds, and Fish of this World, but in a Heavenly form, clarity, and nature, as well as all sorts of Trees, Sprouts, and Flowers: but just as it arises, so it also passes away again: for it is not incorporated together like the Angels, but it figures itself thus in the birth of the rising **dignities** <term>original: "waardigheden." These are the distinct qualities, powers, or "sources" of the divine nature that interact to create spiritual reality.</term> in the Nature-spirit.

When a figure is imaged in a Spirit so that it exists, and if the other Spirit wrestles with this one and overcomes it, then it is again separated or changed, all according to the nature of the dignity; and that is in God like a Holy command.

<meta>This page continues the comparison between the physical world and the spiritual realm, emphasizing that while heavenly forms resemble earthly ones (trees, animals), they are made of divine power rather than perishable matter. Therefore, the creatures—such as animals, birds, fish, and worms—in this world were not created for an eternal existence, but for a perishable one, just as the figures of the Heavens also pass away.

You must not think that in the **Divine Pomp** <term>original: "Goddelyken Pomp." This refers to the majestic, ceremonial manifestation or the "grand procession" of God's glory.</term> animals, worms, or creatures appear in the flesh as they do in this world; no, but I mean only the wonderful proportion, power, and suitability within them.

Now, when I write of trees, sprouts, and fruits, you must not understand this in an earthly sense as in this world, for that is not my meaning—that a dead, hard, wooden tree or stone, consisting of earthly substance, would grow in Heaven. No, but my meaning is Heavenly and Spiritual, yet truly and essentially so; I mean no other thing than what I put down in the letter <note>The author is insisting that these spiritual things are "real" and "literal" in their own realm, even if they aren't made of physical wood and stone.</note>.

The **Heavenly Salitter** <term>original: "Hemelse Salitter." A term borrowed from chemistry (saltpeter) by mystical thinkers like Jacob Böhme to describe the fundamental, "explosive" creative power and divine substance of God's nature.</term> or powers interacting with one another, bring forth heavenly, joyful fruits and colors, all kinds of trees and sprouts, upon which grows the beautiful and lovely Fruit of Life. Likewise, within these powers, all kinds of flowers arise with beautiful heavenly colors and scents; their taste is manifold, each according to its worth and nature, most holy, divine, and joyful. For every dignity bears its own fruit, just as in this **corrupt murder-den** <note>original: "verdorven moordkuil." A stark 17th-century metaphor for the fallen material world, viewed as a place of decay and strife compared to the harmony of heaven.</note> and the dark valley of the Earth all kinds of trees, sprouts, flowers, and fruits arise—including beautiful gemstones, silver, and gold within the Earth, which are examples of the heavenly birth.

Yet you shall know this: that in one place a certain **Quality** <term>original: "Qualiteit." In the author's system, this refers to one of the seven fundamental "source-spirits" or forces of nature that interact to create reality.</term> suddenly shows itself more powerful than in another; then the second triumphs, then the third, then the fourth, then the fifth, then the sixth, then the seventh. And it is thus an eternal wrestling, working, and friendly rising up of Love, wherein through this rising, the Divinity becomes continuously more wonderful and incomprehensible, and—

<meta>catchword: on-</meta>

<summary>This page explains that heavenly forms like trees, animals, and gold are spiritual realities made of divine power rather than physical matter. It describes the "Heavenly Salitter" as the source of celestial life and depicts the interaction of divine qualities as a joyful, eternal "wrestling" of love.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Pomp, Heavenly Salitter, spiritual biology, seven qualities, Fruit of Life, heavenly birth, Jacob Böhme</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the description of the heavenly realm, moving from the physical appearance of trees and fruits to the sensory and spiritual experience of the divine atmosphere. It explores how the movement of the "Fountain-Spirits" and the "Lightning of Life" creates a kaleidoscope of colors and sounds, comparing the glory of the Godhead to a sea of precious gemstones.</meta>

<page-num>188</page-num>
# Of the Eternal Fatherland,

...appears more unfathomable, so that the Holy Angels cannot rejoice enough, nor walk within it enough, and cannot sing that beautiful **WE PRAISE THEE, O GOD** <note>original: "T E D E U M L A U D A N U S." This refers to an ancient Latin hymn of praise, the "Te Deum," traditionally used in liturgy to celebrate great joy or victory.</note> enough, according to the dignity of the great God, and according to His wonderful revelation, wisdom, beauty, colors, fruit, and forms. For these excellencies ascend eternally and always in such a way that with them there is no beginning, nor middle, nor end.

Now, when the Lightning of Life—that is, the **Son of God**—rises in the middle circle within the Fountain-Spirits of God and reveals Himself triumphantly, the Holy Spirit also ascends triumphantly upward. In this ascending, the Holy Trinity also rises in the Heart of the Prince <note>likely referring to Christ or the central governing power of the divine realm</note>, and each one also triumphs according to its nature and quality.

In this ascending, the entire Heavenly Host and all the Angels become triumphant and rich with joy, and the beautiful **WE PRAISE THEE, O GOD** rises up. In this ascending of the Heart, radiance is awakened in the Heart as well as in the entire <term>Salitter</term> <note>A mystical term for the fundamental, "explosive" creative substance or "soil" of the divine world.</note> of Heaven. Then, there arises within the Godhead the wonderful and beautiful imaging of Heaven in many kinds of colors and natures, and each Spirit reveals itself particularly, in its own form.

I can compare it to nothing but the most noble stones, such as **Chrysolite** <note>original: "Cherubin." In historical lapidary lists, this often refers to Chrysolite or a stone associated with the angelic order.</note>, Emeralds, **Chalcedony** <note>original: "Delfin," likely referring to a greenish-blue variety of chalcedony.</note>, Onyx, Sapphire, Diamond, Jasper, Hyacinth, Amethyst, Beryl, Sardius, Carbuncle, and the like.

In such colors and nature, the Natural-Heaven of God reveals itself in the ascending of the Spirits of God: when the Light of the **Son of God** shines therein, it is like a clear sea of the aforementioned colors of the stones.

Oh! If only we humans had feathers and could write this, according to our knowledge, into the Spirit of your souls! How many would then turn back from Sodom and Gomorrah <note>A biblical reference to fleeing from sin and worldly destruction.</note>,

<meta>catchword: morra,</meta>

<summary>This page describes the ecstatic praise of the Angels and the vibrant, gemstone-like colors produced by the movement of the Holy Trinity within the divine substance. The author uses the imagery of precious stones and the "Lightning of Life" to convey the unfathomable beauty of the heavenly realm.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Trinity, Angels, Te Deum Laudamus, Salitter, precious stones, Son of God, Fountain-Spirits, Sodom and Gomorrah</keywords>

<page-num>189</page-num>
<header>and its Joy.</header>
<sig>So</sig>

[Go]morrah <note>The OCR begins mid-word, likely completing "Gomorra" from the previous page, referencing the biblical city of vice.</note>, out of Babel <note>In this context, Babel represents the confused, materialistic, and fallen world of man.</note>, and the greedy, proud valley of tears, which is but anguish and affliction, full of fear, pain, and terror. 

If we travel together along this narrow path of fleshly birth to the green meadows where the <term>wrath of God</term> <note>original: "toorn Gods." In Boehmist thought, "wrath" is a specific spiritual "quality" or "fire" that characterizes the fallen world, which must be overcome to reach the "light."</note> does not reach, then we shall surely rejoice over the suffering we have endured. It matters not if we must now be the World’s Fool, and let the Devil and the power of God’s wrath rush over us; it will serve us more beautifully in the next life than if we had worn a royal crown in this one. For our time here is very short—it is not even worthy of being called "time" at all. Amen.

# CLOSING HYMN

> *See, Brothers, that is the Fatherland*
> *To which our souls journey,*
> *After life's bond is dissolved,*
> *And the years of sighing are over.*
> *Who would not leave the world*
> *For such a Kingdom?*
> *Is the earthly not merely dung and mire,*
> *And worthy of hatred?*
> *That is the beautiful Brotherhood*
> *That we shall find there,*
> *To bind our friendship eternally*
> *Upon the highest step,*
> *And to play with her in the Holy Light*
> *As children of God,*
> *Before the Fatherly Face,*
